Pointwise purity, derived Satake, and Symplectic duality
Victor Ginzburg

Abstract
. It has been known for a long time that Ext \Ext ’s between IC {\operatorname{IC}} -sheaves may often be expressed in terms of Hom \Hom ’s between cohomology groups. We prove a more general result under weaker assumptions. The result is used to describe the action of the derived Satake equivalence on ! ! -pure objects and show that the equivalence enjoys a new kind of functoriality with respect to morphisms of reductive groups. We find and prove normality of the symplectic dual X ! X^{!} for many smooth affine Hamiltonian G G -varieties X X , including X = T ∗ ​ ( G / H ) X=T^{*}(G/H) for all connected reductive subgroups H ⊆ G H\subseteq G . We also describe the symplectic duals 𝐌 ! {\mathbf{M}}^{!} in the case of Coulomb branches and prove that 𝐌 ! {\mathbf{M}}^{!} has symplectic singularities.



1.
Main results

1.1.
Pointwise purity

In this paper, we use the yoga of ‘weights’ either in the étale setting of

[
BBD
]
or in the sense of mixed Hodge modules of Saito
[
Sa
]
.
Given a connected linear algebraic group
G
and a
G
-variety
X

in either of these settings,
there is a mixed equivariant derived category

D_{G}^{\text{mix}}(X)
, often viewed
as the mixed derived category of the stacky quotient
X/G
, cf.
[
HL
, 4.2]

and
[
Ach
]
.
Accordingly, we let

{\Bbbk}
, the coefficient field, be
either
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
or
{\Bbbk}=\mathbb{C}
, respectively.

Fix a stratification

X=\sqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\,X_{\lambda}
by locally closed smooth

G
-stable connected subvarieties where
\Lambda
is a finite labeling set.
We say that
{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{G}^{\text{mix}}(X)

is
*
-
pure
, resp.
!
-
pure
,
if for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
and
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, the
m
-th cohomology sheaf

{\mathscr{H}}^{m}\jmath^{*}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}
, resp.
{\mathscr{H}}^{m}\jmath^{!}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}
,
where
\jmath_{\lambda}:X_{\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,X
is the imbedding
is geometrically constant and
pointwise pure of weight
m
. We say that
{\mathcal{F}}
is pure if it is both
*
-pure and
!
-pure.
We write
X^{G}\subseteq X
for the fixed point set.

Below, we consider the case where the group is a split torus
T

and write
{\mathbb{H}}(\text{-})={H_{T}^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}}(\text{-})
for
T
-equivariant cohomology.
Let
X
be a not necessarily connected projective
T
-variety
with irreducible connected components
and
X=\sqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\,X_{\lambda}
a stratification as above by
T
-stable subvarieties.
Let
{\mathfrak{i}}:Y\,\hookrightarrow\,X
be the closed imbedding of a
T
-stable subvariety
and

Y_{\lambda}:=Y\cap X_{\lambda}
. Thus,
we have
Y=\sqcup_{\mu\in\Sigma}\,Y_{\mu}
, where

\Sigma=\{\mu\in\Lambda\mid Y_{\lambda}\neq\emptyset\}
.
We consider
*
-pure, resp.
!
-pure and pure,
objects of
D_{T}^{\text{mix}}(X)
, resp.
D_{T}^{\text{mix}}(Y)
,
with respect to the stratification
X=\sqcup_{\lambda}X_{\lambda}
,
resp.
Y=\sqcup_{\mu}Y_{\mu}
.

We make the following
assumptions:

S1:

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda
there is a vector space
V_{\lambda}
with a linear
T
-action
and a
T
-equivariant isomorphism
f_{\lambda}:{\kern 5.0pt}X_{\lambda}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}V_{\lambda}
such that

f_{\lambda}(Y_{\lambda})
is a vector subspace of

V_{\lambda}
if
\lambda\in\Sigma
.

S2:

For all
\lambda\in\Sigma
one has
(X_{\lambda})^{T}=\{x_{\lambda}\}

where
x_{\lambda}:=f_{\lambda}^{-1}(0)
.

S3:

For every
\lambda\in\Sigma
there is a Zariski open
T
-stable subset
U_{\lambda}\subseteq X
and a

{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
-action on
U_{\lambda}
that commutes with the
T
-action and
contracts

U_{\lambda}
to the point
x_{\lambda}
.

Given a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded algebra
A
and a pair

M=\oplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\,M_{k},\ N=\oplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\,N_{k}
, of graded
A
-modules,
we let
\Hom^{n}_{A}(M,N)
denote the group
of
A
-linear maps
f:M\to N
such that
f(M_{k})\subseteq N_{k+n}
for all
k
.

Our main general result on pointwise pure sheaves, to be proved in Sect.
4.2
, reads

Theorem 1.1.1
.

If conditions S1-S3 hold then
for any
pure
{\mathcal{E}}_{X}\in D_{T}^{\text{mix}}(X)
and
!
-pure
{\mathcal{F}}_{Y}\in D_{T}^{\text{mix}}(Y)

the functor
{\mathbb{H}}(\text{-})
induces an isomorphism of
graded
{\mathbb{H}}(X)
-modules

\Ext^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{D_{T}(X)}({\mathcal{E}}_{X},{\mathfrak{i}}_{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{Y})\ {\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\ \Hom^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{\mathbb{H}}(X)}({\mathbb{H}}({\mathcal{E}}_{X}),\,{\mathbb{H}}({\mathfrak{i}}_{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{Y})).

By duality, from the theorem one obtains, cf. (
4.3.10
):

Corollary 1.1.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}\in D_{T}^{\text{mix}}(X)
be pure,
\lambda\in\Sigma
and
\iota_{\lambda}:\{x_{\lambda}\}\,\hookrightarrow\,X

the imbedding. Then there is a natural isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}(\iota_{\lambda}^{!}{\mathcal{E}}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\Hom^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{\mathbb{H}}(X)}({\mathbb{H}}(\{x_{\lambda}\}),\,{\mathbb{H}}({\mathcal{E}})).

In the special case where
Y=X
and
{\mathcal{F}}
is both
!
-pure
and

*
-pure
the isomomorphism of the theorem has been proven in
[
BY
]

following
the strategy used in
[
Gi
]
in the nonequivariant setting, cf. also
[
CMNO
]

for a related result.
Some of the arguments used in
op cit

do not work in our more general setting and a nonequivariant analogue of
Theorem
1.1.1

is false, in general. Indeed, let
X
be a projective
T
-variety with
isolated fixed points which is not rationally smooth and let
{\mathcal{E}}:={\operatorname{IC}}(X)
.
Then, the dimensions of the groups
{\mathbb{H}}(\iota_{\lambda}^{!}{\operatorname{IC}}(X))

are not
the same for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
On the other hand, for any
\lambda
, the non-equivariant cohomology group

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}(\{x_{\lambda}\})

is a one dimensional vector space isomorphic to
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}(X)/H^{>0}(X)

as an
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}(X)
-module.
Therefore, the space
\Hom^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}(X)}\big(H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}(\{x_{\lambda}\}),\,H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}({\operatorname{IC}}(X))\big)
is independent of
\lambda
. Thus,
a non-equivariant analogue of the isomorphism of Corollary
1.1.2
,
hence of Theorem
1.1.1
,
does not hold.

Remarks 1.1.3
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
It follows from conditions S1-S2 that one has
\{x_{\lambda},\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}\subseteq X^{T}

and
\{x_{\lambda},\,\lambda\in\Sigma\}=Y^{T}
.
The inclusion
\{x_{\lambda}\}\subseteq(X_{\lambda})^{T}

is not required to be an equality for
\lambda\in\Lambda\smallsetminus\Sigma
.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
Condition S2 is equivalent to the following condition:

S2’:

Each connected component of the fixed point variety
X^{T}
either
does not meet
Y
or
meets
Y
at a single point
x_{\lambda}
and in this case one must have

(X_{\lambda})^{T}=\{x_{\lambda}\}
.

This condition holds trivially if
X^{T}=\{x_{\lambda},\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
, so condition S2
hasn’t appeared in earlier works on the subject where
T
-fixed points in

X
were assumed to be isolated.

{\sf{(iii)}}\;
The set
U_{\lambda}
that appears in condition S3 is not required to contain the stratum
X_{\lambda}
.

Our results on derived Satake rely on Theorem

1.1.1
in the case where

X
is (a sufficiently large projective subvariety of) the affine Grassmannian

{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
of a reductive group
G
and
Y={\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\,\hookrightarrow\,X={\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}

is the imbedding of the affine Grassmannian of a
reductive subgroup of
G
.
Conditions S1-S3 hold for such an imbedding,
Sect.
6.3
.
The pure sheaf
{\mathcal{E}}_{X}
is going to be the regular perverse sheaf on
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
.
Most of the
!
-pure sheaves
{\mathcal{F}}_{Y}
that we will consider are either not

*
-pure or are objects of an ind-completion, in which case the notion of
*
-purity is
not well defined.

1.2.
Derived Satake functor on
!
-pure objects

Below, we will frequently encounter actions of group schemes.
Let
\mathcal{J}
be a group scheme over a scheme
S

and
X,Y
a pair of schemes over
S

equipped with
\mathcal{J}
-actions
\mathcal{J}\times_{S}X\to X,\,\mathcal{J}\times_{S}Y\to Y
.
If
X
is a
\mathcal{J}
-torsor we will use the notation
X\times_{S}^{\mathcal{J}}Y

for the quotient of

X\times_{S}Y
by the diagonal
\mathcal{J}
-action, that is, a twist of
Y
by the
torsor
X
.

Let
G
be a split connected reductive group either over
\mathbb{C}
or

{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Let
{\check{G}}
be the Langlands dual group over
{\Bbbk}
with Lie algebra

{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Let
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
be the set of regular elements,

\varpi:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}:={\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}/\!/{\check{G}}
the coadjoint quotient, and

J=J_{\check{G}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}
the group scheme of
universal centralizers.

Fix a maximal unipotent subgroup
{\check{U}}\subset{\check{G}}
and
a nondegenerate character
\psi:{\check{\mathfrak{u}}}=\Lie{\check{U}}\to\Bbbk
. Let

{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}:=T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})={\check{G}}\times_{\check{U}}(\psi+{\check{\mathfrak{u}}}^{\perp})
be
the Whittaker cotangent bundle on
{\check{G}}/{\check{U}}
.
This is a smooth affine symplectic variety equipped with commuting Hamiltonian
actions of
{\check{G}}
and
J
with moment maps

\pi:{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}},\,(g,\xi)\mapsto\Ad g(\xi)
and
p=\varpi{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}\pi:{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\xrightarrow{\pi}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\xrightarrow{\varpi}{\mathfrak{c}}
, respectively.
The map
\pi
, resp.
p
, makes
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}
a
{\check{G}}
-equivariant
\varpi^{*}J
-torsor,
resp.
{\check{G}}
-torsor.

It will be convenient to view
{\mathfrak{c}}
as a variety equipped with the trivial
J
-action.
There is a canonical isomorphism
{\mathfrak{c}}/J\cong{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}/{\check{G}}
of quotient stacks,
hence an equivalence
\qcoh({\mathfrak{c}}/J)=\qcoh^{J}({\mathfrak{c}})\cong\qcoh({{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}/{\check{G}})=\qcoh^{{\check{G}}}({{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}})
,
between the respective equivariant categories of
quasi-coherent sheav-es.
This equivalence can be described more explicitly as follows.
The pull-back
p^{*}M
of
M\in\qcoh^{J}({\mathfrak{c}})
comes equipped with
a
{\check{G}}\times J
-equivariant structure induced
by the
{\check{G}}\times J
-action on
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}
. There is also an
action
\gamma:J\times_{\mathfrak{c}}\,p^{*}M\to p^{*}M
induced by
the
J
-action on
M
.
We define a new
J
-equivariant structure on
p^{*}M
by twisting the original

J
-equivariant structure by
\gamma
.
The resulting sheaf
descends to a
{\check{G}}
-equivariant sheaf
{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M)
on
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}/J\cong{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
and
the equivalence
\qcoh^{J}({\mathfrak{c}}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\qcoh^{{\check{G}}}({{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}})
sends
M
to

{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M)
.

An important special case is
M=\mu_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
where

X
is an affine Poisson scheme and
\mu:X\to{\mathfrak{c}}

is the moment map for a Hamiltonian

J
-action on
X
.
In this case,
{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M)
is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
-algebra
and the relative spectrum of that algebra
is a quasi-affine scheme
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X):={\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\times_{\mathfrak{c}}^{J}X
that may be viewed as a
twist of
X
by the
\varpi^{*}J
-torsor
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
.
The scheme
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)
comes equipped
with a Poisson structure and a Hamiltonian
{\check{G}}
-action with moment map
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
.
If
X
is a smooth symplectic variety then so is
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)
.

Write

{\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}
for the algebra
\sym{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}

viewed as a dg algebra with zero differential and
such that
\sym^{i}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}
is assigned degree
2i
.
Let

D^{\check{G}}_{\text{perf}}({\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])})
be the
perfect derived category of
{\check{G}}
-equivariant dg
modules over
{\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}
, resp.
(\sym{\check{\mathfrak{g}}},{\check{G}})\mmod

the full subcategory of
graded
{\check{G}}
-equivariant
\sym\mathfrak{g}
-modules, equipped with monoidal structure

(\text{-}){\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{{\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}}(\text{-})
, resp.
(\text{-})\otimes_{\sym{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(\text{-})
.
For any dominant weight
\lambda

one has a free
{\check{G}}
-equivariant
\sym\mathfrak{g}
-module

{\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}\otimes V_{\lambda}
, where
V_{\lambda}
is an irreducible representation of
{\check{G}}
with highest
weight
\lambda
.

Let
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}={G}(K)/{G}(O)
be the affine Grassmannian where
K
, resp
O\subseteq K
,
is the field of formal Laurent, resp. power, series
either over
\mathbb{C}
or
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Let
D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
be the equivariant derived
Satake category equipped with convolution monoidal structure.
Bezrukavnikov and Finkelberg
[
BF
]

established a monoidal
equivalence, the derived Satake equivalence:

(1.2.1)

D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}D^{\check{G}}_{\text{perf}}({\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}),\hskip 10.00002pt{\operatorname{IC}}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})\mapsto{\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}\otimes V_{\lambda}.

It will be more convenient for our purposes to use
a slightly different equivalence
\Phi

introduced in
[
BFN2
, Sect. 5(ii)]
.
For
{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
, let
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})\in(\sym{\check{\mathfrak{g}}},{\check{G}})\mmod
denote the cohomology of
\Phi({\mathcal{F}})
. We also consider
the equivariant cohomology
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}})
,
a finitely generated module over the algebra
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{G}(O)}({\operatorname{pt}})\cong\Bbbk[{\mathfrak{c}}]
.
Let
{\mathcal{H}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})\in\qcoh^{\check{G}}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*})
, resp.

{\mathcal{H}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}})\in\coh({\mathfrak{c}})
, denote
an associated quasi-coherent sheaf on
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
,
resp. coherent sheaf on
{\mathfrak{c}}
.
The construction of
[
YZ
, Sect. 5.1-5.3]
equips the sheaf
{\mathcal{H}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}})

with a
J
-equivariant structure.

We say that an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
is
!
-pure, resp. pure,
if
{\mathcal{F}}
has a lift

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\in D^{\text{mix}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
which is
!
-pure, resp. pure,
with respect to the stratification by the
{G}(O)
-orbits, cf. Sect.
5.1
for more details.

Theorem 1.2.2
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
is
!
-pure then the dg module
{\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})
is formal, i.e,
quasi-isomorphic to its cohomology equipped with zero differential,
and there is a canonical isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}{\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})\cong\jmath_{*}{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{H}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}}))

in
\qcoh^{{\check{G}}}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*})
, where
\jmath:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
is the open imbedding and
\jmath_{*}

a nonderived pushforward.

The theorem shows that the Satake image of a
!
-pure object

{\mathcal{F}}
is completely determined by
the
{G}(O)
-equivariant cohomology of
{\mathcal{F}}
and that
the adjunction
{\mathcal{H}}{\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})\to\jmath_{*}\jmath^{*}{\mathcal{H}}{\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})

is an isomorphism.

Remarks 1.2.3
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
The functor
M\mapsto\jmath_{*}{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M)
is a right adjoint of the functor of Kostant-Whittaker reduction considered in
[
BF
]
, cf. also
[
Ga
]
, Sect. 2.3 and
[
GG
]
, Sect. 3.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
Most of the results of this paper have ‘quantum analogues’ for
the category
D_{{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\ltimes G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
that includes equivariance with respect to loop rotation.
The proofs in the quantum setting are essentially the same as in the classical setting.
However, the statements of quantum analogues of theorems of the paper
involve nil DAHA and related bialgebroid structures introduced in
[
GG
]
. Therefore,
we decided to omit discussion of the quantum case in the present paper.

1.3.
Ring objects

We will consider commutative ring objects
{\mathcal{F}}
of an ind-completion
\text{Ind}D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})

with respect to
!
-restrictions, cf. eg.
[
BFN2
, Sect. 2(i)]
and also Sect.
5.1
below.
If
{\mathcal{F}}
is
!
-pure, hence equivariantly formal,
the cohomology

{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})
, resp.
H_{G(O)}^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}({\mathcal{F}})
,
has the structure of a supercommutative
algebra, by the equivariant Künneth formula. Thus, there is an affine

J
-scheme

\Spec H_{G(O)}^{\text{even}}({\mathcal{F}})
over
{\mathfrak{c}}
, resp.
{\check{G}}
-scheme

\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})
over
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
.
Theorem
1.3.2
below gives a
sufficient condition for these schemes be
of finite type in terms of the
restriction of
{\mathcal{F}}
to the affine Grassmannian of a maximal torus
T\subseteq G
.
The scheme
\Spec H_{G(O)}^{\text{even}}({\mathcal{F}})

is often easier to study than
\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})
.
Theorem
1.3.2
provides a construction
of
\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})
in terms of
\Spec H_{G(O)}^{\text{even}}({\mathcal{F}})

which allows, in many cases, to derive algebro-geometric properties
of the former from similar properties of the latter.

Let
\rho:{H}\to{G}
be a morphism of reductive groups
and
\rho_{\mathrm{Gr}}:{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\to{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}

an induced
ind-proper morphism.
Let
\rho^{\dagger}
be the composite of the following functors

D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{{G}})\ \xrightarrow{{\kern 4.09723pt}\text{Obl}^{{G}(O)}_{{H}(O)}{\kern 4.09723pt}}\,D_{{H}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{{G}})\ \xrightarrow{\;\rho_{\mathrm{Gr}}^{!}\;}\ D_{{H}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{{H}}).

It was observed in
[
BFN2
, Sect. 2(vi)]
in a special case
and proved in
[
Ma
]
in full generality
that the functor
\rho^{\dagger}
has a lax monoidal structure, in particular,
it sends ring objects to ring objects.
The notion of
!
-purity (as opposed to
*
-purity) makes sense for ind-objects
and
\rho^{\dagger}
takes

!
-pure objects to
!
-pure objects.

To state Theorem
1.3.2
,
let
{\mathbb{X}}=\mathbb{X}_{*}({T})
be the cocharacter lattice of
T

and

{\mathbb{X}}_{+}\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}
the dominant Weyl chamber.
The natural imbedding
\varepsilon:{\mathbb{X}}\cong{\mathrm{Gr}}_{T}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}

identifies
{\mathbb{X}}
with the
T
-fixed point set
({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})^{T}
.
For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Ind D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
, we write
\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}

for the stalk of
\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{T(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{T})
at
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}
, so

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T}(\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
.
If
{\mathcal{F}}
is a commutative
ring object then
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})

is an
{\mathbb{X}}
-graded supercommutative algebra and we define
an
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded subalgebra of
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})

as follows

(1.3.1)

H(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}:=\mbox{$\bigoplus$}_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\ H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T}(\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}).

Let
\bar{X}:=\Spec\Bbbk[X]
denote the affine closure
of a quasi-affine scheme
X
.

Theorem 1.3.2
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Ind D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
is a
!
-pure commutative
ring object such that the algebra
H(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}
is finitely generated
then
\Spec H^{\text{even}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})

are schemes of finite type and
there is an isomorphism of
{\check{G}}
-schemes over
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
:

\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})\,\cong\,\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(\Spec{H^{\text{even}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{F}})})}.

In particular,
\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})
is
reduced irreducible and normal
if so is
\Spec H^{\text{even}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{F}})
.

Remarks 1.3.3
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
A supercommutative algebra
A=A^{\text{even}}\oplus A^{\text{odd}}

is finitely generated if and only if
A^{\text{even}}
is a finitely generated algebra and

A^{\text{odd}}
is a finitely generated
A^{\text{even}}
-module.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
All ring objects
{\mathcal{F}}
that will appear in applications considered in this paper
come from ring objects of

D_{{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\ltimes G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
, the
{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\ltimes G(O)
-equivariant derived category where
{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
acts by loop
rotation. In that case the cohomology groups
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{F}})
acquire
a natural Poisson
structure that will appear without further references
in some of the statements in subsequent sections.

Example 1.3.4
.

Let
\jmath_{\lambda}:{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathrm{Gr}}

be the locally closed imbedding of the
G(O)
-orbit associated with

\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, and
{\Bbbk}_{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}}
the constant sheaf on
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}
.
The direct sum
{\mathcal{F}}:=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,(\jmath_{\lambda})_{*}{\Bbbk}_{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}}

has the natural structure of a ring object.
Combining Theorem
1.3.2
with the main result of
[
GK
]
, it is not difficult to deduce
that the algebra
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})
is isomorphic to a subalgebra
of
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]
generated by the algebras
\sym{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}
and
p^{*}\Bbbk[{\check{G}}/{\check{U}}]
,
where
p:T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})\to{\check{G}}/{\check{U}}
is the projection.

1.4.
Organization of the paper

In Section
2

we state Theorem
2.1.2
which relates derived Satake equivalences
\Phi_{H}
and
\Phi_{G}

for a morphism
H\to G
of reductive groups. The proof of Theorem
2.1.2

will be given in Section
6.3
. In Section
3

we discuss applications of Theorem
2.1.2
to symplectic duality
introduced in the fundamental work
[
BZSV
]
.
In Sect.
3.1
we
give a description of the symplectic dual of
T^{*}(G/H)

for any connected reductive subgroup
H\subseteq G
.
The main result of Section
3.2
is Theorem
3.2.4
on the structure
of the symplectic dual
\mathbf{M}^{!}_{G}
of a symplectic representation
\mathbf{M}
.
The theorem provides a description of
\mathbf{M}^{!}_{G}
in terms of the corresponding Coulomb
branch
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},G}
; it follows that
\mathbf{M}^{!}_{G}

is an irreducible normal variety; furthermore,

\mathbf{M}^{!}_{G}
has symplectic singularities if
\mathbf{M}=T^{*}{\mathbf{N}}
is of cotangent type.
We also relate our results to the results
of Gannon-Webster
[
GW
]
and Teleman
[
T
]
;
in Remark
3.2.5
we indicate a
connection with works of
Braverman-Kazhdan
[
BK1
]
,
[
BK2
]
.
The proof of Theorem
3.2.4
is given in Sect.
6.4
.
In Sect.
3.3
we consider the ‘universal Coulomb branch’.
Section
4
is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.1.1
.
In Section
5
we review the necessary material on derived Satake
and the functor
\Phi
. In particular, Sect.
5.3

contains a simple construction
of commutativity
constraint for convolution on the abelian Satake category, which seems to be new.
In Sect.
5.4
we discuss functoriality for universal centralizers
based on a description of the universal centralizer given in
[
DG
]
,

[
BFM
]
, and
[
Ng1
]
.
Theorem
1.2.2
is proved in Section
6.1
modulo
a criterion for finite generation of the cohomology algebra of a

!
-pure ring object. The criterion is proved in Section
7
.

1.5.
Acknowledgements

I thank Tom Gannon for
discussions and for providing proof of Corollary
7.1.6
.
Tsao-Hsien Chen kindly shared with me a draft of
[
Ch
]
and attracted my attention
to the work of Macerato
[
Ma
]
.
I am grateful to Nick Rozenblyum for explaining a general approach to
proving formality of dg objects based on
\infty
-categories which is used in the
proof of Theorem
1.2.2
.
Above all, I am grateful to Misha Finkelberg for
reading parts of a preliminary draft of the paper and for many discussions
on derived Satake and Coulomb branches which have played an important role
in this project.

2.
Functoriality for derived Satake

2.1.

We write
\Lie J_{\check{G}}
for the Lie algebra of the
group scheme
J_{\check{G}}
.
This is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}
-module
with zero Lie bracket which is canonically isomorphic
to the cotangent sheaf
T^{*}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}
,
[
BF
, Sect. 2.6]
,

[
GK
, Lemma 5.1.5]
.
Let
{\mathcal{R}}_{G}:=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,{\operatorname{IC}}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})\otimes V^{*}_{\lambda}

be the regular perverse sheaf, a ring object of
\Ind D_{G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
.

Let
H
and
G
be a pair of connected reductive groups.
To simplify notation we write
S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}={\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}
, resp.

S{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}=\sym({\check{\mathfrak{h}}}[-2])
, and
use subscripts
{\check{H}}
and
{\check{G}}
to distinguish between objects
associated with
{\check{H}}
and
{\check{G}}
, respectively.

Given a morphism
\rho:{H}\to{G}
, we would like to relate
derived
Satake equivalences
\Phi_{G}
and
\Phi_{H}
.
To this end,
observe first that the map
\rho
induces a morphism
from
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}={\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}/\!/{\check{H}}=\mathfrak{h}/\!/H
to
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}={\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}/\!/{\check{G}}=\mathfrak{g}/\!/G
,
and we write
\rho^{*}(\text{-})={\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}(\text{-})

for base change.
Pullback via the differential of the map
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}

gives a morphism

\rho^{*}T^{*}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}\to T^{*}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}
that may be viewed
as a morphism

\rho^{*}\Lie J_{\check{G}}\to\Lie J_{\check{H}}

of abelian Lie algebras. The latter morphism
can be exponentiated to a morphism
\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}\to J_{\check{H}}

of group schemes over
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}
, cf. Sect.
5.4
.

Next, let
\varrho:{\check{H}}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
be
the graph imbedding
h\mapsto(h,\rho(h))
. Then, we have the graph
imbedding
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}
and the above construction yields
a morphism
\varrho^{*}J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}\to J_{\check{H}}

of group schemes over
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}
.
The action of
J_{\check{H}}
on itself by translations, resp. on
{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}
,
induces, via the morphism of group schemes, a
\varrho^{*}J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}
-action
on
J_{\check{H}}
, resp.
{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}
.
Alternatively, one may view
J_{\check{H}}
, resp.
{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}
,
as a
J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}
-scheme over
{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}

via the graph imbedding
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}
.

Following V. Lafforgue
[
La
]
, we define

(2.1.1)

{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}:={\mathsf{Wh}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}(J_{\check{H}})\,=\,J_{\check{H}}\times^{\varrho^{*}J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}}_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}\varrho^{*}{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}\,=\,T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\rho^{*}T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}}),

where we have
used the same symbol
\psi
for a nondegenerate character of
either the group
U_{\check{H}}
or
U_{\check{G}}
.
By construction,
{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}
is a quasi-affine smooth symplectic variety
equipped with a hamiltonian action of the group
{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
with moment map
{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}_{r}^{*}\times{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
.

Theorem 2.1.2

(Functoriality for derived Satake)
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
For any morphism
\rho:H\to G
of connected reductive
groups,
there is an isomorphism of functors

\theta_{H\to G}:\,(\Phi_{H}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}\rho^{\dagger})(\text{-})\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\,\Phi_{G}(\text{-}))^{\check{G}}

that makes
the following
diagram commute:

\textstyle{\Ind D_{G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\dagger}}
\textstyle{\ \Ind D_{H(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{H}}
\textstyle{\Ind D^{\check{G}}_{\text{perf}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\kern 4.09723pt}(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\,\text{-})^{\check{G}}{\kern 4.09723pt}}
\textstyle{\ \Ind D^{\check{H}}_{\text{perf}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}).\ }

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
The algebra

\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]
, resp.
\Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}\times_{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\rho^{*}{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]
,
is a finitely generated integrally closed domain
and there is an isomorphism

(2.1.3)

\displaystyle\Spec{\mathrm{H}\Phi}_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{{G}})

\displaystyle\cong\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}},{\kern 5.0pt}\text{resp.}

(2.1.4)

\displaystyle\Spec H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})

\displaystyle\cong\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(J_{\check{H}})},

of affine Poisson varieties with a Hamiltonian
{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
-action,
resp.
{\check{G}}
-action.

{\sf{(iii)}}\;
The isomorphism of functors in (i) is compatible with composition as follows.
Let
{G_{1}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{12}}{G_{2}}\xrightarrow{{\rho_{23}}}{G_{3}}

be morphisms of connected reductive groups
and
\rho_{13}:=\rho_{23}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}{\rho_{12}}
. Then,
there is a
{\check{G}}_{1}\times\check{G_{3}}
-equivariant dg algebra isomorphism

\theta_{\text{alg}}:\ (\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{2}}\,\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{2}}\to{G_{3}}}])^{{\check{G}}_{2}}\ {\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\ \Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}],

where the algebra
\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}]
is equipped with zero differential,
such that for all
{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{G_{3}}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G_{3}})
the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\big(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{2}}\,(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{2}}\to{G_{3}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,\Phi_{G_{3}}({\mathcal{F}}))^{{\check{G}}_{3}}\big)^{{\check{G}}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\text{alg}}\otimes{\operatorname{Id}}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{Id}}\otimes\theta_{{G_{2}}\to{G_{3}}}}
\textstyle{(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,\Phi_{G_{3}}({\mathcal{F}}))^{\check{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}}
\textstyle{\big(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{2}}\,\Phi_{G_{2}}({\rho_{23}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})\big)^{{\check{G}}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}}
\textstyle{\Phi_{G_{1}}(\rho_{12}^{\dagger}{\rho_{23}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}).}

Remarks 2.1.5
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
The variety
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}
was considered
by Lafforgue
[
La
]
in connection with Langlands functoriality in the dual setting of a morphism
{\check{H}}\to{\check{G}}
rather than
H\to G
.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
If
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}

is a commutative ring object then the isomorphism in (i)
may be interpreted as an isomorphism

(2.1.6)

\Spec\Phi_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})\,\cong\,\big(\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}\ \mathrel{{\mathop{\times}\limits}}_{\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}}\ \Spec\Phi_{G}({\mathcal{F}})\big)/\!/{\check{G}},

of affine
derived
schemes.
If
{\mathcal{F}}
comes from a ring object of
D_{{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\ltimes G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})

by forgetting loop rotation then the right hand side of the above isomorphism
may be viewed as a derived
Hamiltonian reduction with respect to the diagonal action
of
{\check{G}}
, where the sign of the
Poisson structure on the factor
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}
is changed.
Such an isomorphism agrees
with expectations coming from the QTFT considered by Moore and Tachikawa

[
MT
]
, cf. also
[
KRS
]
.
If
{\mathcal{F}}
is
!
-pure then the objects
\Phi_{G}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\Phi_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})

are formal, therefore, the
derived scheme in the right hand side of (
2.1.6
)
is a nonderived (super)-scheme with coordinate
ring
(\mathrm{Tor}^{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}({\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}],\,{\mathrm{H}\Phi}_{G}({\mathcal{F}})))^{\check{G}}
.

{\sf{(iii)}}\;
In the special case where
H
is a Levi subgroup of
G

isomorphism (
2.1.4
) was proved by Macerato
[
Ma
]
.
Our isomorphism is, in general, different from the one in
[
Ma
]

since our functor
\rho^{\dagger}
doesn’t involve
shearing
.
This is because we construct (
2.1.4
) by describing the image of
the adjunction
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}(\rho_{!}\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\to H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})

and this map is incompatible with shearings. If

H=T
is the maximal torus then we have
J_{\check{T}}=T^{*}({\check{T}})
and

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(J_{\check{T}})}\cong\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})}
.
In this case, (
2.1.4
) is equivalent to an algebra isomorphism

f:H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})]
.
For a simply connected group
G
the isomorphism has been
constructed earlier in

[
GK
]
using the same map
f
.
On the other hand, the isomorphism constructed in
[
Ma
]
(in the more
general case of an arbitrary Levi) is given by a slightly different map
\tilde{f}

which agrees with parabolic restriction, as in
[
GR
]
.
The map
\tilde{f}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}f^{-1}
is an automorphism
of the algebra
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})]
given by
the action of a certain element of
{\check{T}}\times{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
, where the

{\check{T}}
-action, resp.
{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
-action, is induced by the
{\check{T}}
-action on

{\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}}
by right translations, resp.
{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
-action by dilations
along the fibers of the cotangent bundle.

{\sf{(iv)}}\;
Theorem
2.1.2
has a quantum counterpart
for the derived Satake category with loop rotation.
The algebra
\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]
has a natural quantization defined as follows,
cf.
[
La
, Remark 2-h]
.
Let
Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})
be the center
of the asymptotic enveloping algebra
U_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})
and

{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{\ell}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})
the
{\Bbbk[\hbar]}
-algebra of asymptotic Whittaker differential operators
on
{\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}}
,
cf. eg.
[
GG
, Sect. 3.3]
for more details and notation. There are also algebras
Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{h}}})
and

{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{r}}_{\hbar}({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})
defined in a similar way.
The morphism
H\to G
induces a homomorphism

Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})\to Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{h}}})
, so we get the composite algebra
homomorphism
Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})\to Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{h}}})\to U_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{h}}})\to{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{r}}_{\hbar}({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})
.
The desired quantization of
\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]
is
a version of
quantum Hamiltonian reduction (cf.
[
GK
, Example 1.1.2(ii)]
)
defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{H\to G}\ =\ {\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{r}}_{\hbar}

\displaystyle({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})\,\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})}^{{\mathsf{Sph}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}})}\,{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{\ell}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})\ \cong\ \big({\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{r}}_{\hbar}({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})}{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{\ell}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})\big)^{Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})}

\displaystyle:=\ \{u\otimes u^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{r}}_{\hbar}({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})}{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{\ell}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})\mid(zu)\otimes u^{\prime}=u\otimes(u^{\prime}z)\ \forall z\in Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})\}.

Here,
{\mathsf{Sph}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}})\cong H^{{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}}_{{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\ltimes G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})

is the
spherical
nil DAHA
and in the second line we have used Sweedler notation

u\otimes u^{\prime}=\sum_{i}\,u_{i}\otimes u^{\prime}_{i}
.
The spherical nil DAHA has the structure of a
cocommutative
bialgebra and
the tensor product

{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{r}}_{\hbar}({\check{H}}/U_{\check{H}})\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})}{\mathscr{D}}^{\psi_{\ell}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})

has the natural structure of a
{\mathsf{Sph}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}})
-comodule,

[
GG
, Proposition 3.3.4]
. The symbol
\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}})}^{{\mathsf{Sph}}_{\hbar}({\check{G}})}

stands for
balanced product
,
[
GG
, Definition 2.1.1]
.

\lozenge

Here are a few examples of the variety

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}
for some natural imbeddings
\rho:H\,\hookrightarrow\,G
:

Cartesian product:

Let
L
be a reductive group
with Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{l}}
and

p:{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{L}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{L}}
the natural map,
cf. Sect.
1.2
. Let
G:=H\times L

and
\rho:H\,\hookrightarrow\,H\times L,\,h\mapsto(h,1_{L})
,
the imbedding. Then, we find

{\mathsf{Wh}}_{H\to G}={\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times\{0\}}^{J_{\check{H}}\times J_{\check{L}}}({\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}\times{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{L}})\,=\,({\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}^{J_{\check{H}}}{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}})\times(p^{-1}(0))/J_{\check{L}}.

It is known, cf. e.g.
[
BFN2
, Sect. 5]
, that

\overline{\,{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}^{J_{\check{H}}}{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}\,}\cong T^{*}{\check{H}}
(Moore-Tachikawa
isomorphism). Further, writing
{\mathcal{N}}_{\check{L}}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{l}}}^{*}
for the nilpotent cone, one has

(p^{-1}(0))/J_{\check{L}}\cong{\mathcal{N}}_{\check{L}}\cap{\check{\mathfrak{l}}}^{*}_{r}
,
the principal nilpotent orbit.
Hence,
\overline{(p^{-1}(0))/J_{\check{L}}}\cong{\mathcal{N}}_{\check{L}}
.

We conclude that

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}}_{H\to G}}\cong T^{*}{{\check{H}}}\times{\mathcal{N}}_{\check{L}}
,
and we obtain

(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{H\to G}]{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\;\text{-})^{\check{G}}=(\Bbbk[T^{*}{\check{H}}]\otimes\Bbbk[{\mathcal{N}}_{\check{L}}]){\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}\otimes S{\check{\mathfrak{l}}}}\;\text{-})^{{\check{H}}\times\check{L}}=(\Bbbk[{\mathcal{N}}_{\check{L}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{l}}}}\;\text{-})^{\check{L}}.

Sicilian theory:

Let
\rho:G\,\hookrightarrow\,G^{n}=G\times G\times\ldots\times G
(
n
factors) be the diagonal imbedding.
Then, we have

\rho^{\dagger}_{{\mathrm{Gr}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{G^{n}}={\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}\ldots{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{G}
.
In this case, the existence of the variety
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G\to G^{n}}}
was conjectured
by Moore and Tachikawa
[
MT
]
.
It follows from Theorem
2.1.2
that we have

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G\to G^{n}}}\cong\Spec{\mathrm{H}\Phi}(\rho^{\dagger}_{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}}{\mathcal{R}}_{G^{n}})\cong\overline{T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})^{\times_{\mathfrak{c}}(n+1)}/J_{n}},

were,
T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})^{\times_{\mathfrak{c}}(n+1)}:=T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}\ldots\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})
(
n+1
factors)
and
J_{n}
is the kernel of the multiplication map

J_{\check{G}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}\ldots\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}J_{\check{G}}\to J_{\check{G}},\,(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n+1})\mapsto\gamma_{1}\cdots\gamma_{n+1}
.
The proof of the above
isomorphism was found in
[
BFN2
, Sect. 5]
.
The fact that the algebra
\Bbbk[T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})^{\times_{\mathfrak{c}}n}/J_{n-1}]

is finitely generated was proved later by
Arakawa
[
A
]
using representation theory of
affine
\mathcal{W}
-algebras.

Levi subgroups:

Let
H\,\hookrightarrow\,G
be the imbedding of a Levi subgroup
of a parabolic subgroup
P\subseteq G
. Let
U_{\check{P}}
be the unipotent radical
of

{\check{P}}
. Then
it was shown by Gannon
[
Ga
]
(and predicted in
[
La
]
)
that one has

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}\cong\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{P}})}
.
The action of
{\check{H}}
in

\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{P}})}
is induced by the action of
{\check{P}}/U_{\check{P}}

on
{\check{G}}/U_{\check{P}}
by right
translations.
Using the isomorphism

{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]
of Theorem
2.1.2
(ii)
(which, in this case, easily follows from

[
Ma
]
), we obtain
\Spec{\mathrm{H}\Phi}_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{P}})}
.

Theta correspondence (following V. Lafforgue):

Let
(V,\omega)
,
resp.
(E,\beta)
, be a symplectic, resp. quadratic, vector space,
so the vector space
E\otimes V
has the natural symplectic structure.
In either of the following two cases one has
{\check{H}}=SO(E)
and
{\check{G}}=Sp(V)
:

(1):

H=SO_{2n}\,\hookrightarrow\,G=SO_{2n+1}
, where
2n=\dim E=\dim V
;

(2):

H=SO_{2n+1}\,\hookrightarrow\,G=SO_{2n+2}
, where
2n=\dim E-2=\dim V
.

In these cases, by
[
La
]
there is an isomorphism

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}\cong E\otimes V
such that the moment map is the natural map

E\otimes V\to{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\cong\wedge^{2}E\times\sym^{2}V
given by

\mbox{$\sum_{i}$}\,e_{i}\otimes v_{i}\,\mapsto\,\big(\mbox{$\sum_{k<\ell}$}\,\omega(v_{k},v_{\ell})e_{k}\wedge e_{\ell},\;\mbox{$\sum_{k,\ell}$}\,\beta(e_{k},e_{\ell})v_{k}v_{\ell}\big).

From Theorem
2.1.2
(ii)
and the isomorphism
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{P}})]

in the Levi case above, changing the notation from
{\check{G}}
to
G
, we deduce

Corollary 2.1.7
.

Let
P
be a parabolic of a connected
reductive group
G
and
U_{P}
the unipotent radical of
P
. Then
the algebra
\Bbbk[T^{*}(G/U_{P})]

is finitely generated.∎

Remarks 2.1.8
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
The statement of the above corollary was previously known
in the case where
P
is a Borel subgroup,
[
GR
]
,
and in the case of an arbitrary parabolic of
a group of type
A
. In type
A
, the varieties
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}
that arise either from
Sicilian theories or Levi subgroups
may be realized as Coulomb branches,
see
[
BFN2
]
, resp.
[
GW
]
. The finite generation of Coulomb branches
has been proved in
[
BFN1
]
.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
Let
{\check{B}}\subseteq\check{P}
be a Borel subgroup,

{\mathfrak{u}}_{\check{P}}=\Lie U_{\check{P}}
, and
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\check{P}}^{\perp})

the projectivization of the vector space
{\mathfrak{u}}_{\check{P}}^{\perp}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
.
By a recent result
[
FL
, Theorem 1.6(i)]
,
Corollary
2.1.7
implies that the variety

\overline{{\check{G}}\times_{\check{B}}{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\check{P}}^{\perp})}

is a Mori dream space.

{\sf{(iii)}}\;
According to
[
FL
, Theorem 1.6(ii)]
, the variety

\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{P}})}
has
symplectic singularities if and only if
{\check{G}}\times_{\check{B}}{\mathbb{P}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\check{P}}^{\perp})

is of
Fano type
in the sense of
op cit
.
In the special case where
\check{P}=\check{B}
is a Borel subgroup,

\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{B}})}
has
symplectic singularities, by
[
Ga
]
.
Also, it was shown by Bellamy
[
Bel
]
that all Coulomb branches have
symplectic singularities.

Question 2.1.9
.

Does
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}
have symplectic singularities ?

\lozenge

The moment maps
\mu_{{\check{H}}}
and
\mu_{{\check{G}}}
for the actions in
{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}

of
{\check{H}}
and
{\check{G}}
, respectively, fit into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}_{r}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \varpi_{\check{H}}\ }
\textstyle{\ {\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \mu_{{\check{H}}}\ }
\scriptstyle{\ \mu_{{\check{G}}}\ }
\textstyle{\ {{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \varpi_{\check{G}}\ }
\textstyle{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \rho\ }
\textstyle{\ {\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}

The image of the map
\mu_{{\check{H}}}\times\mu_{{\check{G}}}
, resp.
\mu_{{\check{G}}}
, equals

{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}_{r}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
, resp.
\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}={\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
.
We obtain a chain of maps

(2.1.10)

{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}\ \xrightarrow{\ \widetilde{\mu}\ }\ {\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}_{r}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\ \xrightarrow{\ p_{2}\ }\ \rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\ \xrightarrow{\ \rho^{*}\varpi_{\check{G}}\ }\ \rho^{-1}({\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}})\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}},

where the map
\widetilde{\mu}
is induced by
\mu_{{\check{H}}}\times\mu_{{\check{G}}}

and
p_{2}
is the second
projection. The following statements may be helpful
for understanding the geometry of
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}
:

(2.1.11)

\bullet{\kern 5.0pt}
The map

\tilde{\mu}
is an
{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
-equivariant
\widetilde{\varpi}^{*}J_{\check{H}}
-torsor,
where
\tilde{\varpi}=\rho^{*}\varpi_{\check{G}}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}p_{2}
;

(2.1.12)

\bullet{\kern 5.0pt}
The map
p_{2}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}\tilde{\mu}
is a
{\check{G}}
-equivariant
{\check{H}}
-torsor.

(2.1.13)

\bullet{\kern 5.0pt}
There are isomorphisms

\;{\overline{\mu_{\check{G}}}}^{-1}({{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}})\cong{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}\cong({\check{H}}\times{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}})/\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}
,
where

\overline{\mu_{\check{G}}}:\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
is an extension of

\mu_{\check{G}}
to the affine closure and
{\check{H}}
, resp.
{\check{G}}
,

acts on the first, resp. third, factor of

{\check{H}}\times{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
.

(2.1.14)

\bullet{\kern 5.0pt}
We have an equality
\dim{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}=(\dim{\check{H}}+\rk{\check{H}})+(\dim{\check{G}}-\rk{\check{G}}).

\bullet{\kern 5.0pt}
There is an isomorphism
of
{\check{H}}
-varieties, resp.
{\check{G}}
-varieties:

(2.1.15)

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}/\!/{\check{G}}\cong\Spec\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}]^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}
, resp.
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}/\!/{\check{H}}\cong{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}.

\bullet{\kern 5.0pt}
We have

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}/\!/({\check{H}}\times{\check{G}})\cong{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}
; therefore,

\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}
is a hyperspherical
{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
-variety.

Here, the
second isomorphism in (
2.1.13
) is proved by choosing
a Kostant slice in
{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}_{r}
, resp.
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
,
which provides an isomorphism
{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{H}}\cong{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times{\check{H}}
, resp.

{\mathsf{Wh}}_{\check{G}}\cong{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}\times{\check{G}}
.
Other claims in (
2.1.11
)-(
2.1.15
), with the exception of the
second isomorphism in (
2.1.15
), easily follow from (
2.1.10
)
and we omit the proofs.

Proof of the second isomorphism in (
2.1.15
).

Let

\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}:={\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
and
let
pr_{1}:\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}
, resp.
pr_{2}:\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\to{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
, be the first, resp. second, projection.
Since

{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}\to\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}

is an
{\check{H}}
-torsor, one has

\Bbbk[\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}}/\!/{\check{H}}]\cong\Bbbk[\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}]
.
Thus, proving the desired isomorphism amounts to showing that the
natural map
\overline{\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}\to\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}

is an isomorphism.
To this end, observe that the morphism

pr_{1}
is obtained from the adjoint quotient
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}

by base change. Hence
pr_{1}
is a flat morphism such that
the restriction of
pr_{1}
to
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
is smooth.
It follows that

\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
is a generically reduced complete intersection
in
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\times{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
of dimension
\dim\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}=\dim{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}+(\dim{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}-\dim{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}})
.
We deduce that
\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
is reduced and
the codimension of

\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\smallsetminus\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
in

\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
is
\geq 2
, since a similar inequality holds for the codimension of

{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\smallsetminus{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
in
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
.
It follows that

{\mathcal{O}}_{\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}}
is a maximal Cohen-Macaulay sheaf.
This implies that the adjunction
{\mathcal{O}}_{\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}}\to j_{*}j^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}}
,
where
j:\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\,\hookrightarrow\,\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
is the open imbedding, is an isomorphism.
Hence,
\Gamma(\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},\,{\mathcal{O}}_{\rho^{*}{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}})\to\Gamma(\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}},\,{\mathcal{O}}_{\rho^{*}{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}})

is an isomorphism.∎

Observe that
the map
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}
may be viewed as a map from the set of
coadjoint orbits in
{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}_{r}^{*}
to the set of coadjoint orbits in
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
. Lafforgue
interpreted this map in terms of Hamiltonian geometry. Specifically,
we see from (
2.1.11
)-(
2.1.12
) that
for any coadjoint orbit
\Omega\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}_{r}

there is a unique coadjoint orbit
\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
such that

\mu_{\check{H}}^{-1}(\Omega)=\mu_{\check{G}}^{-1}(\Omega^{\prime})
and
the map
p_{2}:\mu_{\check{G}}:\mu_{\check{H}}^{-1}(\Omega)\to\Omega^{\prime}
is an
H
-torsor
that induces an
isomorphism
\mu_{\check{H}}^{-1}(\Omega)/{\check{H}}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\Omega^{\prime}

of hamiltonian
{\check{G}}
-varieties. The assignment
\Omega\mapsto\Omega^{\prime}

provides the desired hamiltonian
interpretation for the map between
the sets of coadjoint orbits in
{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}^{*}_{r}
and
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
, respectively.

3.
Symplectic duality

3.1.

Let
{N}
be
a smooth affine
G
-variety,
{N}(K)
the
formal loop space, resp.
{N}(O)\subset{N}(K)
the formal arc space.
In the so-called placid case, specifically, assuming the conditions
given in
[
BZSV
, Sect. 7.3.1]
hold, it is possible
to define the dualizing sheaf
of
{N}(O)
and
the
basic object

i_{*}\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)}
,
where we write
i

either for the imbedding

N(O)\,\hookrightarrow\,N(K)
or the induced morphism of stacks
G(O)\backslash N(O)\,\hookrightarrow\,G(O)\backslash N(K)
.
Associated with the basic object, there is a commutative
ring object
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}={\underline{\mathcal{E}^{\!}\textit{nd}}}_{{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}}(i_{*}\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)})\in\Ind{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
, an internal endomorphism object with respect to the action
of the monoidal category
{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
on
D_{G(O)}(N(K))
,
[
BZSV
, 8.1]
.
It follows from definitions that one has an isomorphism

\Phi_{G}({\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G})=\RHom_{D_{G(O)}(N(K))}(i_{*}\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{G}\star i_{*}\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)}).

Let
M=T^{*}N
. The scheme
M_{G}^{!}:=\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G})
,
resp.
{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}:=\Spec H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{A}}_{M,{G}})
,
is usually referred to as the
symplectic dual
, resp.

Coulomb branch
, of
M
.
These schemes
come equipped with a Poisson structure and
a Hamiltonian action of
{\check{G}}
and
J_{\check{G}}
, respectively.

Remark 3.1.1
.

In
[
BZSV
]
, the authors also considered a suitably defined Whittaker setting
where
T^{*}N
is replaced by certain twisted versions
T^{*}(N,\Psi)
.

There is an equivalent, more explicit construction of the ring
object
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}
in terms of the scheme
{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}

referred to as the
variety of triples
in
[
BFN1
]
, resp.
relative Grassmannian

in
[
BZSV
, 8.2]
. The scheme
{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}
,
which is a formal loop version of the
finite dimensional Steinberg variety,
is defined by the cartesian square in the diagram

(3.1.2)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{G(K)\times_{G(O)}{N}(O)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{G}}
\scriptstyle{pr_{1}}
\textstyle{G(K)/G(O)={\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}}
\textstyle{{N}(O)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{{N}(K)}

where the map
a_{G}
is the action

(g,n)\mapsto g.n
.
Let
\omega_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}}
be the dualizing sheaf and
q_{G}=pr_{1}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}f_{1}:\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}\to{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
.
Placidity assumtions, see
[
BZSV
, Sect. 8]
,
insure that there is a well-defined
(renormalized) pushforward
(q_{G})_{*}\omega_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}}
and there is an isomorphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}\cong(q_{G})_{*}\omega_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}}
. It follows that

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{A}}_{N,{G}})\cong H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}(q_{G})_{*}\omega_{{\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G}})\cong H^{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},G})
, see
[
BFN2
, Sect. 2(i)-(ii)]
in a special case
and
[
BZSV
]
in general.

Given a morphism
\rho:H\to G
of reductive groups one may view the
G
-variety

{N}
as an
H
-variety. It was shown in
[
BFN2
]
(in the special case
where
{N}
is a representation of
G
; the
same argument works in general, cf.
[
BZSV
, Remark 8.3.2]
)
that

{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},H}\cong\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}
.

We prove the following

Proposition 3.1.3
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
The sheaf
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}

is
!
-pure if and only if the Borel-Moore homology

H^{{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}}_{T(O)}({\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},T})
is pure, where
T
is
a maximal torus of
G
.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
If
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}
is
!
-pure and the algebra
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}({\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},T})_{+}

is finitely generated
then the following holds:

(1)

The scheme
{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}
is a scheme of finite type flat
over
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}
;

(2)

The scheme
M^{!}_{G}
is
of finite type and there are isomorphisms

M_{G}^{!}\,\cong\,\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}})},{\kern 5.0pt}\ \text{resp.}{\kern 5.0pt}\ M_{G}^{!}/\!/{\check{G}}\,\,\cong\,\,{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}.

Proof.

Part (i) is a direct consequence of
the isomorphism

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G})\cong H^{{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}}_{T(O)}({\mathcal{Z}}_{{N},T})
and our definition of
!
-purity.
All statements in (ii) except for the isomorphism

M_{G}^{!}/\!/{\check{G}}\,\,\cong\,\,{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}

are consequences of Theorem
1.3.2
. To prove the isomorphism
observe that for any
!
-pure sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
, by Theorem
1.2.2
,
one has canonical isomorphisms

{\mathrm{H}\Phi}_{G}({\mathcal{F}})^{\check{G}}\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,\Gamma\big({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},\,\jmath_{*}{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{H}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}}))\big)^{\check{G}}\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,\Gamma({\mathfrak{c}},{\mathcal{H}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{F}})^{J}).

It is clear that if
{\mathcal{F}}
is a commutative ring object then these
are algebra isomorphisms. Applying this to
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G}

we obtain

\Bbbk[M_{G}^{!}]^{\check{G}}={\mathrm{H}\Phi}_{G}({\mathcal{A}}_{{N},G})^{\check{G}}\cong\Bbbk[{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}]^{J_{\check{G}}}=\Bbbk[{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}]
,
as desired.
∎

Remarks 3.1.4
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
From the isomorphism
M_{G}^{!}/\!/{\check{G}}\,\cong\,{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}

we see that if

{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}

is a finite morphism then the algebra
\Bbbk[M_{G}^{!}]^{\check{G}}
is finitely generated as a
\Bbbk[{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}]^{\check{G}}
-module.
The latter property forces the Poisson bracket on
\Bbbk[M_{G}^{!}]^{\check{G}}
vanish.
It follows that if
M_{G}^{!}
is an irreducible normal affine variety,
then this variety is hyperspherical if and only if the morphism
{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}

is finite.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
The isomorphism
M_{G}^{!}/\!/{\check{G}}\,\cong\,{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}/\!/J_{\check{G}}

also implies that the natural map

\Bbbk[{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}]=(\sym{\check{\mathfrak{g}}})^{\check{G}}\to{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})^{\check{G}}
is an isomorphism if and only if
general
J_{\check{G}}
-orbits are dense in
{\mathcal{C}}_{M,{G}}
.

{\sf{(iii)}}\;
It is expected that in some cases where the affine
G
-variety
N

is singular the role of the dualizing sheaf

\omega_{N(O)}
in the definition of the basic object is played
by the sheaf
{\operatorname{IC}}(N(O))
, where the
{\operatorname{IC}}
-sheaf is
defined using a result of Grinberg-Kazhdan and Drinfeld.
Assuming this
{\operatorname{IC}}
-sheaf is pure one might expect the ring object

{\mathcal{A}}_{N,G}
be
!
-pure. If that is the case then the isomorphisms of
Proposition
3.1.3
(2) hold for the singular variety
N
.

\lozenge

In the case where
{N}=G/H
for a connected reductive subgroup
H\subseteq G
,
there is an isomorphism
of stacks
G(O)\backslash{\mathcal{Z}}_{G/H,G}\cong H(O)\backslash{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}

such that the map
q_{G}
corresponds to the morphism

H(O)\backslash{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\to G(O)\backslash{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
induced by the
imbedding
\rho:H\,\hookrightarrow\,G
,
[
BZSV
, 8.2.2]
.
It follows that
H^{{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}}(G(O)\backslash{\mathcal{Z}}_{G/H,\,G})

\cong H^{{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}}(H(O)\backslash{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})\cong\Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}]
. Thus, there is a
natural isomorphism

{\mathcal{C}}_{T^{*}(G/H),\,{G}}\cong J_{\check{H}}.

Further, it was shown by Chen
[
Ch
, Sect. 4.1]
that for
N=G/H
,
using the notation of diagram (
3.1.2
),
one has

(3.1.5)

\displaystyle\RHom_{D_{G(O)}(N(K))}(i_{*}

\displaystyle\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{G}\star i_{*}\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)})

\displaystyle\cong\RHom_{D_{G(O)}(N(O))}(\omega_{G(O)\backslash{N}(O)},\,(f_{2})_{*}q_{G}^{!}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong R\Gamma_{H(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H},\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G}),

It follows that there is an algebra isomorphism

\Bbbk[T^{*}(G/H)^{!}_{G}]\cong H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})
.

Corollary 3.1.6
.

There is an isomorphism
of
{\check{G}}
-schemes, resp.
J_{\check{G}}
-schemes:

T^{*}(G/H)^{!}_{G}\,\cong\,\overline{J_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\rho^{*}{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}},\ {\kern 5.0pt}\text{resp.}{\kern 5.0pt}\ T^{*}(G/H)^{!}_{G}/\!/{\check{G}}\,\cong\,\overline{J_{\check{H}}/\!/\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}.

Moreover,
T^{*}(G/H)^{!}_{G}
is an irreducible normal variety.

Proof.

The first isomorphism is a consequence of (
3.1.5
) and (
2.1.4
).
The second isomorphism follows from algebra isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\rho^{*}{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]^{\check{G}}

\displaystyle\ \cong\ {\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}\rho^{*}{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]^{{\check{G}}\times\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\ \cong\ {\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}\rho^{*}({\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}/\!/{\check{G}})]^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}

\displaystyle\ \cong{\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}]^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\ \cong\ \Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}]^{\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}}\ =\ \Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}/\!/\rho^{*}J_{\check{G}}].\qed

Remark 3.1.7
.

In the case of affine spherical varieties
G/H
the isomorphisms of the corollary
have been conjectured, and proved in several small rank cases,
by Devalapurkar, cf.
[
Dev
, (3.14)]
.

3.2.
Coulomb branches

In this subsection we assume that
\Bbbk
is the field of complex numbers
since we are going to use exponential functions.

Let
{G}\to Sp(\mathbf{M})
be
a representation in a symplectic vector space
\mathbf{M}

that satisfies the ‘anomaly cancelation’
condition from
[
BDFRT
]
.
In
op cit
the authors used
{\mathscr{D}}
-modules to associate
to the representation
\mathbf{M}
a ring object
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}\in\Ind{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
.
The anomaly cancelation automatically
holds in the ‘cotangent case’ where
\mathbf{M}=T^{*}{\mathbf{N}}={\mathbf{N}}\oplus{\mathbf{N}}^{*}
and
the representation in
\mathbf{M}
is a direct sum
of a representation
G\to GL({\mathbf{N}})
and the dual representation in
{\mathbf{N}}^{*}
.
In that case
the construction of
[
BDFRT
]

reduces to earlier constructions of
[
Na
]
,
[
BFN1
]
,
[
BFN2
]
.

Teleman
[
T
]
, cf. also
[
GW
, Sect. 2.1]
,
associated to a representation
\rho:G\to GL({\mathbf{N}})

a certain
W
-invariant rational section
{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\to{\check{T}}
,
It was essentially stated in
loc cit
,
and follows from the description of
the universal centralizer due to
[
DG
]
,
[
BFM
]
, cf. also

[
Ng1
, Proposition 2.2.3]
, which will be
recalled in Sect.
5.4
below,
that this section defines a rational section

\epsilon_{\rho}:{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}\dashrightarrow J_{\check{G}}
.
Translation by
\epsilon_{\rho}
gives a birational automorphism
\tau_{\rho}:J_{\check{G}}\dashrightarrow J_{\check{G}},\,j\mapsto\epsilon_{\rho}\cdot j
,
where
j\mapsto\epsilon_{\rho}\cdot j
denotes group multiplication in the group scheme
J_{\check{G}}
.
Thus, one obtains an automorphism
\tau^{*}_{\rho}
of the field
{\mathbb{C}}(J_{\check{G}})

of rational functions.
Gannon and Webster introduced
a ‘
gluability
’ condition
[
GW
, Definition 2.6]

on the representation
\rho
and proved, generalizing work of Teleman, that if

\rho
is gluable then the coordinate ring of the corresponding
Coulomb branch
\mathbf{M}={\mathbf{N}}\oplus{\mathbf{N}}^{*}
of cotangent type has the following description,

[
GW
, Theorem 2.7]
:

(3.2.1)

\Bbbk[{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}}]\cong\{f\in\Bbbk[J_{\check{G}}]\ \mid\ \tau_{\rho}^{*}(f)\text{ is a
regular function on }J_{\check{G}}\}.

Write

\mu_{\text{left}}
and
\mu_{\text{right}}
for the moment maps

T^{*}{\check{G}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
induced by the action of
{\check{G}}
on itself by left and
right translations, respectively.
Let
{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}:=\{z\in T^{*}{\check{G}}\mid\mu_{\text{left}}(z)=\mu_{\text{right}}(z)\in{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\}
.
This is a smooth locally closed
\Ad{\check{G}}
-stable subvariety of
T^{*}{\check{G}}
.
Using either left or right invariant trivialization
T^{*}{\check{G}}\cong{\check{G}}\times{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}

one obtains

{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}=\{(g,\xi)\in{\check{G}}\times{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}=T^{*}{\check{G}}\mid\Ad g(\xi)=\xi\}
.
The second projection

{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}},\,(g,\xi)\mapsto\xi
, gives
{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}
the structure of an

\Ad{\check{G}}
-equivariant group scheme on
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
called the group scheme of

regular centralizers
.
This group scheme is canonically
isomorphic to the group scheme
\varpi^{*}J_{\check{G}}
, a pullback of
J_{\check{G}}

via the coadjoint quotient
\varpi:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}
.
Therefore, the pullback of
the section
\epsilon_{\rho}
along
\varpi
gives an
\Ad{\check{G}}
-equivariant rational section

\varpi^{*}\epsilon_{\rho}:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\dashrightarrow\varpi^{*}J_{\check{G}}\cong{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}
.
In either left or right invariant trivialization
of
T^{*}{\check{G}}
the map
\varpi^{*}\epsilon_{\rho}

has the form

\xi\mapsto(\gamma_{\rho}(\xi),\xi)
, where

\gamma_{\rho}:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\dashrightarrow{\check{G}}
is a rational map
defined as a composition of
\varpi^{*}\epsilon_{\rho}
and the natural projection

T^{*}{\check{G}}\to{\check{G}}
.
It is not difficult to show that the image of
\varpi^{*}\epsilon_{\rho}

is an
\Ad{\check{G}}
-stable smooth
Lagrangian subvariety
\Lambda_{\rho}\subseteq T^{*}{\check{G}}
.

Let
{X}
be an affine irreducible normal variety with a Hamiltonian
{\check{G}}
-action

{\check{G}}\ni g:x\mapsto g.x
, such that
the moment map

\mu_{X}:{X}\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
is a flat dominant morphism.
Translation by
\gamma_{\rho}

gives a
{\check{G}}
-equivariant birational automorphism

(3.2.2)

\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{X},\rho}:{X}\dashrightarrow{X},{\kern 5.0pt}x\mapsto\gamma_{\rho}(\mu_{X}(x)).x,

such that
\mu_{X}(\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{X},\rho}(x))=\mu_{X}(x)
for all
x\in X
.
Motivated by (
3.2.1
), we define an affine scheme over
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
as follows:

(3.2.3)

{X}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}:=\Spec A_{{X},\rho},{\kern 5.0pt}\text{where}{\kern 5.0pt}A_{{X},\rho}:=\{f\in\Bbbk[{X}]\ \mid\ \boldsymbol{\tau}_{{X},\rho}^{*}(f)\text{ is a
regular function on }{X}\}.

There are
two natural imbeddings
j_{+},j_{-}:{X}\,\hookrightarrow\,{X}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}
, induced by the
algebra maps
\Bbbk[{X}]\to A_{{X},\rho}
which send
f
to
f
, resp.

\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{X},\rho}^{*}(f)
. The scheme
{X}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}
is,
by construction,
the affine closure of a quasi-affine scheme over
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
obtained by gluing
two copies of the variety
X
using
\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{X},\rho}
as
a transition map.
There is a
{\check{G}}
-action on
{X}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}
that restricts, via
j_{+}

and

j_{-}
,
to the
{\check{G}}
-action on
X
.
Similarly, there is a
{\check{G}}
-equivariant morphism

{\mu}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}:{X}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}\to{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
such that
{\mu}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}j_{+}={\mu}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}j_{-}=\mu
.

The proof of the following result will be given in Sect.
6.4
.

Theorem 3.2.4
.

For any
representation

{G}\to Sp(\mathbf{M})
that satisfies the anomaly cancelation condition, one has

{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{odd}}\Phi({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}})=0
and the following holds:

(1)

The scheme
\mathbf{M}_{{G}}^{!}
is an irreducible
normal affine hyperspherical variety and there is an isomorphism

\mathbf{M}_{{G}}^{!}\,\cong\,\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}})}
,
of Poisson varieties with Hamiltonian
{\check{G}}
-action.

(2)

If
{\mathbf{M}={\mathbf{N}}\oplus{\mathbf{N}}^{*}}
is of cotangent type for a representation

\rho:G\to GL({\mathbf{N}})
then the variety
\mathbf{M}_{G}^{!}
has
symplectic singularities.

(3)

If the representation
\rho
in (2)
is gluable then there is an isomorphism

\mathbf{M}^{!}_{\check{G}}\cong{({\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}})}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}
, of
{\check{G}}
-schemes over
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
.

Remarks 3.2.5
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
The isomorphism
\mathbf{M}_{{G}}^{!}\,\cong\,\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}})}
in (1)
has been conjectured by Gannon and Webster

[
GW
, Conjecture A.2]
, cf. also
[
Na2
]
.

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
There is a quantum counterpart of the isomorphism
\mathbf{M}^{!}_{\check{G}}\cong{({\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}})}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}

where the role of
the algebra
\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]

is played by the algebra
{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})
of Whittaker differential operators.
The natural quantization of the Lagrangian subvariety
\Lambda_{\rho}\subseteq T^{*}{\check{G}}

is a very central holonomic
{\mathscr{D}}
-module
\Phi_{\rho}
on
{\check{G}}

considered in

[
GG
, Sect. 1.3]
.
The role of
the automorphism
\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}},\rho}^{*}

is played by the functor of
convolution with
\Phi_{\rho}
.
It was shown in
[
GG
]
that the
{\mathscr{D}}
-module
\Phi_{\rho}
has a canonical central
structure and convolution with
\Phi_{\rho}
is an exact functor.

The
{\mathscr{D}}_{\check{G}}
-module
\Phi_{\rho}
is a counterpart
of the perverse sheaf on
{\check{G}}
, called
\gamma
-
sheaf
,
associated with the representation
\rho
by Braverman and Kazhdan
[
BK2
]
.
In the setting of finite fields, the function on
{\check{G}}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
obtained from
the
\gamma
-sheaf by taking trace of Frobenius
is a generalization of the
\Gamma
-function,
[
BK1
]
.
This may be viewed as
a heuristic ‘explanation’ of the
appearance of (
\hbar
-analogues of)
\Gamma
-functions in

[
T
, Sect. 7]
.
We are going to discuss the construction of the quantization of
\mathbf{M}_{G}^{!}
in terms of
the
{\mathscr{D}}
-module
\Phi_{\rho}
, and its relation to the construction in
loc cit

in more detail elsewhere.

\lozenge

Example 3.2.6

(
The adjoint representation
)
.

Let a reductive group
G
, resp.
{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
, act in
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie G
by the adjoint
action, resp. by dilations.
The Coulomb branch of cotangent type associated
with the resulting representation of the group
G\times{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
in

{\mathbf{N}}=\mathfrak{g}
is an integrable system

\mu_{{\mathfrak{c}}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}}:{\mathcal{C}}_{T^{*}\mathfrak{g},{G}\times{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}

and we have, cf.
[
BFN1
, Remark 6.20]
:

\mu_{{\mathfrak{c}}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}}^{-1}({\mathfrak{c}}\times\{\kappa\})\cong\begin{cases}(T^{*}({\check{T}}))/W&{\kern 5.0pt}\text{if}{\kern 5.0pt}\kappa=0\\
{{\mathcal{CM}}}_{\kappa}({\check{T}},W)&{\kern 5.0pt}\text{if}{\kern 5.0pt}\kappa\neq 0,\end{cases}

where
{{{\mathcal{CM}}}_{\kappa}}({\check{T}},W)
is the
trigonometric
Calogero-Moser
variety with parameter
\kappa
.
Using this and the main result of
[
GK
]
, from
of Theorem
3.2.4
(1) one deduces the following
isomorphism:

\mu_{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}}^{-1}({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\times\{\kappa\})\cong\begin{cases}\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})}/W&{\kern 5.0pt}\text{if}{\kern 5.0pt}\kappa=0\\
\overset{\,}{\overline{T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})\times_{\mathfrak{c}}^{J}{\mathcal{CM}}_{\kappa}({\check{T}},W)}}&{\kern 5.0pt}\text{if}{\kern 5.0pt}\kappa\neq 0.\end{cases}

Here,

\mu_{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}}:(T^{*}\mathfrak{g})^{!}_{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}\times{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
is the moment map,

W
acts on
\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})}
via the Gelfand-Graev action
constructed in
[
GR
]
,
[
GK
]
, and we have used
that for any quasi-affine variety
X
equipped with an action of a finite group

\Gamma
one has
\overline{X/\!/\Gamma}=\Spec\Bbbk[X]^{\Gamma}=\overline{X}/\!/\Gamma
.

3.3.
The universal Coulomb branch

Let
{\mathbf{N}}={\mathsf{st}}
be the defining representation of the group
G=GL({\mathbf{N}})
in a vector space

{\mathbf{N}}
of dimension
n
.
An associated Coulomb branch
\mathbf{M}={\mathsf{st}}\oplus{\mathsf{st}}^{*}

is called the ‘universal Coulomb branch of
cotangent type’,
cf.
[
BDFRT
]
for motivation. We have
{\check{G}}\cong GL_{n}=GL_{n}({\mathbb{C}})
, resp.

{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}={\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}={\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}({\mathbb{C}})
.
The
GL_{n}
-action on
itself by left translations induces
a Hamiltonian action in
T^{*}GL_{n}
. We identify
{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
with
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

via the trace pairing and let
T^{*}GL_{n}\cong GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

be the left invariant trivialization.

The representation
{\mathsf{st}}
is gluable since the
group
GL({\mathbf{N}})
contains the group

{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
of dilations, cf.
[
GW
, Sect. 2.2]
.
From an explicit formula for the section
\epsilon_{\mathsf{st}}
given in
[
T
]
,
[
GW
]
,
it is not difficult to find that the map

\gamma_{\mathsf{st}}:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\to{\check{G}}
equals the restriction to the open set
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}={\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

of the identity map
\xi\mapsto\xi
, the latter being
viewed as a rational map
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\to GL_{n}
with singularities
at the divisor
\det\xi=0
.
It follows that the Lagrangian subvariety

\Lambda_{\mathsf{st}}\subseteq T^{*}GL_{n}
is the diagonal

\Lambda_{\mathsf{st}}=\{(x,\xi)\in GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\mid x=\xi\}
,
alternatively,
\Lambda_{\mathsf{st}}
equals the image of the section

d\Tr:GL_{n}\to T^{*}GL_{n}
of the cotangent bundle, where
d\Tr
is the differential
of the function
GL_{n}\to{\mathbb{C}},\,g\mapsto\Tr(g)
.
The quantization of
\Lambda_{\mathsf{st}}

is the Braverman-Kazhdan
{\mathscr{D}}_{{}_{GL_{n}}}
-module

\Phi_{{\mathsf{st}}}={\mathscr{D}}_{{}_{GL_{n}}}(\Tr)
generated
by this function,
cf.
[
GG
]
.

Next, let the group
{\check{G}}\times{\check{G}}=GL_{n}\times GL_{n}
act on the
vector space
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
by left and right multiplication.
The hamiltonian
GL_{n}
-action on
T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

induced by the action
by left, resp.
right, multiplication
will be referred to as the ‘left’, resp. ‘right’, action.
Let
U\subset GL_{n}
be the maximal unipotent subgroup
of upper-triangular matrices with
1
’s on the diagonal and

T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U
,
resp.
U_{\psi}\backslash\!\backslash\!\backslash T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U
,
the Whittaker reduction of
T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
with respect
to the right action of
U
, resp. left and right action
of
U\times U
.
We identify

GL_{n}
with the subset of
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
of invertible matrices,
which is a
GL_{n}\times GL_{n}
-stable open subset. Thus,
we obtain an open imbedding
T^{*}GL_{n}\,\hookrightarrow\,T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
,
and we will use similar notation for Whittaker reductions
of
T^{*}GL_{n}
; in particular, we have
T^{*}GL_{n}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U={\mathsf{Wh}}_{GL_{n}}
.

Proposition 3.3.1
.

The imbedding
T^{*}GL_{n}\,\hookrightarrow\,T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
, resp.

T^{*}GL_{n}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U\ \,\hookrightarrow\,\ T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U{\kern 5.0pt}\;\text{and}{\kern 5.0pt}\;U_{\psi}\backslash\!\backslash\!\backslash T^{*}GL_{n}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U\ \,\hookrightarrow\,\ U_{\psi}\backslash\!\backslash\!\backslash T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U,

extends to an isomorphism

{(T^{*}GL_{n})}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
, resp.

{({\mathsf{Wh}}_{GL_{n}})}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}\ {\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\ T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U{\kern 5.0pt}\;\text{and}{\kern 5.0pt}\;{(U_{\psi}\backslash\!\backslash\!\backslash T^{*}GL_{n}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U)}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}\ {\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\ U_{\psi}\backslash\!\backslash\!\backslash T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}/\!/\!/_{\psi}U.

Sketch of proof.

We use the natural identification
T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\cong{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

In terms of this identification,
the left, resp. right, action of an element
g\in GL_{n}
is given by the formula

g:(a,b)\mapsto(ga,bg^{-1})
, resp.
g:(a,b)\mapsto(ag^{-1},\,gb)
, and an associated
moment map
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\to{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

is the map
\mu_{\text{left}}:(a,b)\mapsto ab
, resp.
\mu_{\text{right}}:(a,b)\mapsto ba
.
left invariant trivialization sends
(a,b)\in
to
(a,ab)
.
The map
\gamma_{\mathsf{st}}
being the identity map in the
left invariant trivialization

From (
3.2.2
) and the formula for the map
\gamma_{\mathsf{st}}
,
we see that for
X=T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}={\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
,
viewed as a
GL_{n}
-variety with respect to the left action,
the rational map

\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{X},{\mathsf{st}}}
reads

\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{}_{{T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}},{\mathsf{st}}}}:\,{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\dashrightarrow{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}},{\kern 5.0pt}(a,b)\mapsto((ab)a,\,b(ab)^{-1})=(aba,bb^{-1}a^{-1})=(aba,a^{-1}).

Now, the identification of
GL_{n}
with an open subset of
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

induces the identification
T^{*}GL_{n}=GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\subset{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}

(which is different from the identification

T^{*}GL_{n}=GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
provided by the left invariant trivialization).
For any
a\in GL_{n}
the linear map
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\to{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}},\,b\mapsto aba
, is injective,
hence it is an isomorphism. Therefore, the map

GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\to GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}},\,(a,b)\mapsto(a,aba)
, is an automorphism.
Hence, from the displayed formula above we deduce that

\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{}_{{T^{*}{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}},{\mathsf{st}}}}(GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}})={\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times GL_{n}
.
It follows that the complement in
{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
of the union of open
sets
GL_{n}\times{\mathfrak{gl}_{n}}
and
