, Sect. 4.4]
the authors introduce a dg
\infty
-category

{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
of
graded
constructible sheaves which is
obtained from
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{m,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})

by an appropriately defined ‘semisimplification’ of the Frobenius action.
The category
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
comes equipped with an auto-equivalence

{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{F}}\langle 1\rangle
, the Tate twist.
In the special case of the stack
{\operatorname{pt}}_{n}:=\Spec{\mathbb{F}}_{q^{n}}
, the category
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\operatorname{pt}}_{n})

is, by definition, the category
{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}_{c}
of perfect chain complexes of graded

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}
-vector spaces.
Tensor product of complexes gives

{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}_{c}
the structure of a symmetric monoidal category and

{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
has, by construction, the structure of a
{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}_{c}
-module category,
for any
{\mathcal{Y}}_{n}
. This allows to upgrade
\Hom
’s in
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})

to
\Hom
’s enriched over
{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}_{c}
. Thus, for any objects

{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
there is an internal Hom-object

{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\in{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}_{c}
.

In
[
HL
, Sect. 5]
the category
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
was equipped with a perverse t-structure
and with a
weight structure

in the sense of
[
Bon
]
. The latter structure comes from the weight
filtration on mixed sheaves, as in
[
BBD
]
.
Associated with each of these two structures there is
an
\infty
-subcategory
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})^{\heartsuit_{t}}
, resp.
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})^{\heartsuit_{w}}
,
of
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
, the heart. An object
{\mathcal{F}}
of
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
is said
to be pure of weight
m
if
{\mathcal{F}}[-m]\in{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})^{\heartsuit_{w}}
.
There is a natural lift of any
{\operatorname{IC}}
-sheaf of geometric origin to a pure object
of
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
,
[
HL
, Sect. 5]
.
Pure objects of
{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}_{c}
are perfect complexes
V
such that the
i
-th cohomology

H^{i}(V)
is concentrated in grade degree
i
. In that case, one has

V\cong\oplus_{i}\,H^{i}(V)\langle-i\rangle[-i]
.

Now, let
G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
be a split reductive group over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}

and
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
an associated affine Grassmannian. Then,
for each
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
we consider the stack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda}:=G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(O)\backslash{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}

and for each
n
let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda,n}:=({\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda})_{n}
.
The perverse sheaf
{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda}
has a lift
to a pure object
{\mathcal{R}}^{gr}_{\leq\lambda}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda,n})

and the algebra
\Ext^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{D_{G(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda})

has an upgrading

{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda}):=\oplus_{i,j}\,{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda}\langle i\rangle[j])
,
which is a ring object of the category
{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}
.
The purity of
\Ext^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{D_{G(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda})

translates into the statement that
{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda})

is a pure object of
{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}
, hence this object is isomorphic
to

\oplus_{i}\,H^{i}({\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda})\langle-i\rangle[-i]
.
This isomorphism is equivalent to the formality result proved in
[
BF
, Sect. 6.5]
.

A similar argument applies to
!
-pure objects of
D^{mix}_{G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})

as follows.
In general, for any stack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{n}
, the forgetful functor

{\mathrm{Shv}}_{m,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})\to D^{mix}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
is essentially surjective
by the definition of the category
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{m,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
,
[
HL
, Sect. 4.4]
.
Therefore, any
!
-pure object
{\mathcal{F}}\in D^{mix}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})
has a lift to an object of
{\mathrm{Shv}}_{m,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda,n})
.
The image of the
lift under the ‘semisimplification of Frobenius’ functor

{\mathrm{Shv}}_{m,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda,n})\to{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda,n})
, see
[
HL
, Sect. 1.1]
,
yields an object
{\mathcal{F}}^{gr}\in{\mathrm{Shv}}_{gr,c}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\leq\lambda,n})
.
Since
{\mathcal{F}}
is
!
-pure, the group

\Ext^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{D_{G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{F}})\cong H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(O)}({\mathbb{D}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}})
is pure.
Arguing as above, we deduce that the internal Hom-object

{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{F}}^{gr}):=\oplus_{i,j}\,{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{F}}^{gr}\langle i\rangle[j])\in{\mathbb{V}_{\!}\mathrm{ect}}^{gr}
is pure and, therefore, isomorphic to the direct sum

\oplus_{i}\,H^{i}({\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{F}}^{gr})\langle-i\rangle[-i]
.
It follows that
{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{F}}^{gr})
, viewed as a module object over
the ring object
{\mathscr{H}\textit{om}}^{gr}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda})
,
is isomorphic to
\Ext^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{D_{G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{F}})
,
viewed as a graded module over the graded algebra

\Ext^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{D_{G_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})}({\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda},\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\leq\lambda})
.
The desired formality of the dg module
\Phi({\mathcal{F}})\in D^{{\check{G}}}_{\text{perf}}({\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])})

follows from this using that the functor
\Phi
is isomorphic
to the composition of the functor
\RHom({\mathcal{R}},\text{-})
and the functor

{\mathscr{C}}\textit{h}
induced by a Chevalley automorphism, cf. Sect.
5.3
.

To prove the remaining statements of Theorem
1.2.2
,
recall the flat morphism
p:{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}
, cf. Sect.
1.2
.
Since
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}
is an affine variety, for any
M\in\qcoh^{J}({\mathfrak{c}})

one has
\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}\Gamma(M)\cong\Gamma(p^{*}M):=\Gamma({\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}},p^{*}M)
.
If, moreover,
M
is flat over
{\mathfrak{c}}
then, using the proof of
Lemma
5.2.6
, we deduce

\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}^{J}\Gamma(M)\cong\Gamma(p^{*}M)^{{\mathfrak{j}}}\cong\Gamma((p^{*}M)^{J})=\Gamma({{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}},{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M))=\Gamma({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},\,\jmath_{*}{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M)).

We know that
\Phi({\mathcal{R}})={\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}]
by
[
BFN2
, Sect. 5(vii)]
, hence

{\mathsf{H}}(\Phi({\mathcal{R}})\cong\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]
, the Kostant-Whittaker reduction of
{\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}]
,
by
[
BF
, Theorem 2]
. Since the sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
is
!
-pure, the
{\mathsf{C}}
-module

{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
is free and
from Lemma
5.2.6
we obtain

(6.1.1)

{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})\cong{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}})\cong{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{R}})\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}}^{J}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}}).

Hence, taking
M={\mathcal{H}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}})
in the displayed formula above,
we deduce an isomorphism
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})

\cong\Gamma({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*},\,\jmath_{*}{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{H}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{F}})))
,
and the desired isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})\cong\jmath_{*}{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}(M)
follows.
∎

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.2

Recall the notation
{\mathbb{H}}(\text{-})=H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T}(\text{-})
,
resp.
{\mathsf{H}}(\text{-})=H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}(\text{-})
.
The following proposition, which is part of the statement
of Theorem
1.3.2
, will be proved in Section
7
.

Proposition 6.2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\Ind D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
be a
!
-pure commutative
ring object. If
the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}
is finitely generated
then so are the algebras
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})^{W}
and
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
.∎

We now prove Theorem
1.3.2

assuming the proposition.
First, we show that if the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}
is finitely generated
then so is
the algebra
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})
.
Since
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}})
, by Proposition
6.2.1
it suffices to show that
the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{!}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}}))_{+}
is finitely generated.
To this end, write

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}})=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{R}})
,
resp.
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})
.
By
[
GK
]
, there is an
{\mathbb{X}}
-graded algebra
isomorphism

(6.2.2)

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}})=\bigoplus_{\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon_{\nu}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}})\;\cong\;\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]=\bigoplus_{\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]_{\nu}.

Since
\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}})\cong(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}){\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
we obtain
isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}}))\cong{\mathbb{H}}((\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}){\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}))

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\ {\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{R}})\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{\mathrm{S}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\ {\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{R}})\,\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\ \Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]_{\lambda}\,\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}),

of
{\mathbb{X}}
-graded
{\mathrm{S}}
-algebras,
where the isomorphism in the second line holds by Remark
4.3.5
.

We define a
{\check{T}}
-action on
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]
,
resp.
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
, by letting
{\check{T}}
act
in
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]_{\nu}
, resp.
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon_{\nu}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
, via the character

\nu
, resp. an inverse character
-\nu
.
Let
{\check{T}}
act in
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
via the
tensor product action. Then, from the isomorphisms above we obtain
the following
{\mathbb{X}}
-graded, resp.
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded, algebra isomorphism:

(6.2.3)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}}))

\displaystyle\,\cong\,\big(\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})\big)^{\check{T}},{\kern 5.0pt}\text{resp.}

\displaystyle({\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{F}})))_{+}

\displaystyle\,\cong\,\big(\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]_{+}\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}\big)^{\check{T}}.

The algebra
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]_{+}

is finitely generated by
[
GR
]
,
[
Bro
]
, and
the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}

is finitely generated by the assumptions of the theorem.
It follows that
\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{\check{U}})]_{+}\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}
,
hence its subalgebra of
{\check{T}}
-invariants, is finitely generated.
Thus,
{\mathrm{H}\Phi}({\mathcal{F}})
is finitely generated, as desired.

To prove the remaing statements of the theorem,
put
X=\Spec{\mathsf{H}}^{\text{even}}({\mathcal{F}})
and let
\mu:X\to{\mathfrak{c}}
be the structure morphism.
Thus,
X
is an affine irreducible and normal
J
-scheme and we must show that
the scheme
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)=({\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\times_{\mathfrak{c}}X)/J
,
a twist of
X
by
the
\varpi^{*}J
-torsor
\pi:{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
,
is quasi-affine, irreducible and normal.
Using that the torsor
\pi
has an étale local section, cf. eg.

[
GK
, Lemma 3.2.3]
, we deduce that the morphism

\mu\times\pi:{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
is étale locally isomorphic to the
first projection
pr:{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\times_{\mathfrak{c}}X\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
.
This projection is an affine morphism since the scheme
X
is affine.
Since
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
is quasi-affine we deduce that
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)
is quasi-affine.
The variety
X
is irreducible and normal by the assumptions of the theorem.
The morphism
\varpi
being smooth,
it follows by smooth base change
that
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)
is irreducible and normal.

To complete the proof we use that the functors
{\mathsf{H}}
and

{{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}:\qcoh^{J}({\mathfrak{c}})\to\qcoh^{G}({{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}})
are monoidal.
Hence, Theorem
1.2.2
yields algebra isomorphisms

{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})\cong\Gamma({{{\mathcal{W}}h}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}))\cong\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)]
.
We obtain an isomorphim
\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})\cong\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)}
. By the first part of the proof we know that

\Spec{\mathrm{H}}^{\text{even}}\Phi({\mathcal{F}})
is a scheme of finite type.
Thus, it follows from the above that
\overline{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(X)}

is an irreducible and normal algebraic variety, and we are done.
∎

6.3.
Functoriality

In this section we will prove Theorem
2.1.2
, which requires some preparations.

Let
{H}
be a reductive subgroup
of a connected reductive group
{G}
.
Let
T_{H}\subseteq B_{H}\subseteq{H}

be a maximal torus and a Borel subgroup of
{H}
.
We choose (as we may) a maximal torus and a Borel subgroup

T\subseteq B\subseteq{G}

such that
T_{H}\subseteq T
, resp.
B_{H}\subseteq B
.
Let
{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)
be the corresponding
cocharacter lattices.
Our choices insure that

T_{H}={H}\cap T
, resp.

B_{H}={H}\cap B
.
Let
L=Z_{G}(T_{H})
be the centralizer of
T_{H}
,
a Levi subgroup of

{G}
such that
T_{H}
is contained in the center of
L
.
Let
I=\{\gamma\in{G}(O)\mid\gamma(0)\in B\}

be the Iwahori subgroup of
{G}(K)
associated with
B
.
Then, the group

I_{H}:={H}(O)\cap I
is the Iwahori subgroup of
{H}(K)

associated with
B_{H}={H}\cap B
,
and we have
{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})=H(K)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)
.

We identify

{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}
with the image of the closed
imbedding
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{H}}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{G}}
.
Let
{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})^{T}
,
\lambda\mapsto x_{\lambda}
, be the canonical bijection and

I.x_{\lambda}
,
resp.
I_{H}.x_{\lambda}
, the Iwahori orbit of
x_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 6.3.1
.

{{\kern 5.0pt}\sf{(i)}}\;
For any
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)
one has

{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\cap I.x_{\lambda}=\begin{cases}I_{H}.x_{\lambda}&\text{if}\ {\kern 5.0pt}\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})\\
\emptyset&\text{if}\ {\kern 5.0pt}\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)\smallsetminus{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H}).\end{cases}

{\;\sf{(ii)}}\;
Conditions S1-S3 of Sect.
1.1
hold
for
X={\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
, viewed as a
T_{H}
-variety equipped with stratification

{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}=\sqcup_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{G})}\,I.x_{\lambda}
, and the subvariety
Y={\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)
. Then
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\cap I.x_{\lambda}
is an

I_{H}
-stable subvariety of
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}
, hence this subvariety
is a union of
I_{H}
-orbits. Let
I_{H}.x_{\mu}
, where
\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})
,
be such an orbit. Then, we have

x_{\mu}\in I_{H}.x_{\mu}\subseteq I.x_{\lambda}
.
This forces

\mu=\lambda
. It follows that
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})
and
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\cap I.x_{\lambda}=I_{H}.x_{\lambda}
,
proving (i).

Next, we verify condition S1. Let
I^{u}
be the (pro)unipotent radical
of
I
and
I^{u}_{\lambda}
the stabilizer of

x_{\lambda},\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)
in
I^{u}
.
The adjoint action of
T
makes the vector space

V_{\lambda}:=\Lie(I^{u}/I^{u}_{\lambda})
a finite dimensional
representation of
T_{H}
and the map

\Lie(I^{u})\to I.x_{\lambda},\,v\mapsto\exp(v).x_{\lambda}
, induces a

T
-equivariant, hence
T_{H}
-equivariant,
isomorphism
V_{\lambda}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}I.x_{\lambda}
.
Observe further that the group
{H}(O)\cap I^{u}
equals the (pro)unipotent radical

I^{u}_{H}
of the group
I_{H}
. Therefore, for
x_{\lambda},\,\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})
, the stabilizer

I^{u}_{H,\lambda}
of

x_{\lambda}
in
I^{u}_{H}
equals
{H}(O)\cap I^{u}_{\lambda}
.
This gives a
T_{H}
-equivariant imbedding

\Lie(I^{u}_{H}/I^{u}_{H,\lambda})\,\hookrightarrow\,\Lie(I^{u}/I^{u}_{\lambda})=V_{\lambda}
such that
the isomorphism
V_{\lambda}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}I.x_{\lambda}
restricts to
an isomorphism
\Lie(I^{u}_{H}/I^{u}_{H,\lambda}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}I_{H}.x_{\lambda}
.
Thus, an inverse
f_{\lambda}:I.x_{\lambda}\to V_{\lambda}
of
the isomorphism
V_{\lambda}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}I.x_{\lambda}

satisfies condition S1.

To check condition S2, observe that
({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})^{T_{H}}={\mathrm{Gr}}_{L}

and any element of
{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})
is central in
L(K)

since the torus
T_{H}
is contained in the
connected center of
L
.
We know that
x_{\lambda}\in{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}
if and only if
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})
,
by (i). In that case we find

(I.x_{\lambda})^{T_{H}}={\mathrm{Gr}}_{L}\cap I.x_{\lambda}=I_{L}.x_{\lambda}\cong I_{L}.x_{\lambda}.L(O)/L(O)=x_{\lambda}.L(O)/L(O)\cong\{x_{\lambda}\}.

This proves S2.
It is well known that condition S3 holds for affine Grassmannians.
∎

It follows from Lemma
6.3.1
that results of Section
4

are applicable in the above setting.
Write
\rho:{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}

for the
imbedding.
Then, Corollary
4.3.7
implies that if

{\mathcal{F}}\in D^{\text{mix}}_{I_{H}}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})
is
!
-pure and

{\mathcal{E}}\in D^{\text{mix}}_{I_{G}}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})
is pure then
there is a natural isomorphism

(6.3.2)

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}({\mathcal{F}}\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}\,\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{E}}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}({\mathcal{F}})\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{G})}\,H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}({\mathcal{E}}).

We now turn to Satake categories.
We have
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}(\omega_{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}})=H^{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}_{H(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})\cong{\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}]
.
Hence, taking
W_{H}
-invariants in (
6.3.2
) in the case
{\mathcal{F}}=\omega_{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}}

and using (
5.2.1
),
by repeating the proof of Lemma
5.2.6
we obtain the following result

Corollary 6.3.3
.

If
\rho:H\,\hookrightarrow\,G
is an imbedding of connected reductive groups
then for any pure
{\mathcal{E}}\in\Ind{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}

there is a natural isomorphism

(6.3.4)

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{E}})\ {\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\ {\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}]\otimes_{{\Bbbk}[{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}]}^{J_{\check{G}}}H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{E}}).

If
{\mathcal{E}}
is a ring object then this is a ring isomorphism.∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1.2
.

Step 1:  Proof of isomorphism (
2.1.4
).
We factor the morphism
\rho
as a composition

H\xrightarrow{\varrho}H\times G\xrightarrow{pr_{2}}G
.
Then, we have
pr_{2}^{!}{\mathcal{R}}_{G}=\omega_{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{R}}_{G}
. This sheaf is a
!
-pure
ring object and by the Künneth formula we have an isomorphism

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)\times G(O)}(pr_{2}^{!}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong H^{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}_{H(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})\otimes H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})
.
One has an algebra
isomorphism
H^{\textrm{\tiny{BM}}}_{H(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})\cong\Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}]
, resp.

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]:={\Bbbk}[T^{*}_{\psi}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})]
, by
[
BF
]
.
Observe further that one has

(6.3.5)

{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}})\ =\ J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}}^{pr_{2}^{*}J_{\check{G}}}(pr_{2}^{*}{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}})\ \cong\ J_{\check{H}}\times{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}.

We deduce that
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)\times G(O)}(pr_{2}^{!}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong{\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}\times{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]\cong{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}})]
.
This implies isomorphism (
2.1.4
) in the case of the morphism

pr_{2}:H\times G\to G
.

Thus, we are reduced to the case where our
morphism
\rho:H\to G

is an imbedding. In this case
isomorphism (
2.1.4
) is a direct consequence of
(
6.3.4
) for
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{R}}_{G}
.

Step 2:  Proof of part (ii).
To prove isomorphism (
2.1.3
), we compute

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}\Phi}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\ \cong\ H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{H(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{H}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})

\displaystyle\ \cong\ H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{H(O)}}(\varrho^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{H\times G}))

\displaystyle\ \cong\ \Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{H}}}^{\varrho^{*}J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}}}\varrho^{*}({\mathsf{Wh}}_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}})]\,\cong\,{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}],

where the third isomorphism follows from (
2.1.4
) and the last isomorphism is (
2.1.1
).

The algebras
\Bbbk[J_{\check{H}}]
and
{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]
are finitely generated
integrally closed domain.
We deduce that so is
the algebra
{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}(J_{{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}})]\cong{\Bbbk}[J_{\check{H}}]\otimes{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]
, by (
6.3.5
).
To prove that the algebra

{\mathrm{H}\Phi}_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})
is finitely generated we consider the following
commutative diagram of closed imbeddings:

\textstyle{({\mathrm{Gr}}_{H})^{T_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T_{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{H}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}}

By Theorem
1.3.2
(i),
it suffices to show that the algebra

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}(\varepsilon_{H}^{\dagger}\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})_{+}
is finitely generated.
Using that
\varepsilon_{H}^{\dagger}\rho^{\dagger}=i^{\dagger}\varepsilon_{G}^{\dagger}
from (
6.2.2
), we deduce

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}(\varepsilon_{H}^{\dagger}\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})_{+}=H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T_{H}}(i^{\dagger}\varepsilon_{G}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})_{+}=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}(T_{H})_{+}}\,\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{U_{\check{G}}})]_{\lambda},

where
{\mathbb{X}}(T_{H})_{+}
is the dominant Weyl chamber in
{\mathbb{X}}(T_{H})
.
Since
{\mathbb{X}}(T_{H})_{+}
is a finitely generated semigroup
it follows from
[
Bro
]
(and the proof in
[
GR
]
)
that the algebra

\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}(T_{H})_{+}}\,\Bbbk[T^{*}({\check{G}}/{U_{\check{G}}})]_{\lambda}

is finitely generated, as desired.
∎

Step 3:  Proof of part (i).
Write
S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}={\sym({\check{\mathfrak{g}}}[-2])}
.
It is clear that there are isomorphisms

(\text{-}){\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])\cong(\text{-})\otimes_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])\cong(\text{-})\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}]{\kern 5.0pt}\text{and}{\kern 5.0pt}(M\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])^{\check{G}}\cong M,

for any
{\check{G}}
-representation
M
.
Also,
\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G}
is
!
-pure, so
\Phi(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})
is formal
so from Step(2) we obtain an isomorphism

\Phi_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})\cong{\Bbbk}[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]
. Using that

\Phi_{G}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})=S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}]
, by
[
BFN2
, Sect. 5]
, from the above isomorphisms
we find

(6.3.6)

\displaystyle\Phi_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})

\displaystyle\cong\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]\cong(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])^{\check{G}}

\displaystyle\cong(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}]))^{\check{G}}\cong\big(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\Phi_{G}({\mathcal{R}}_{G})\big)^{\check{G}}.

For
V\in\Rep{\check{G}}
, let
Fr(V)=S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes V
, a free
S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}
-module
equipped with the diagonal
{\check{G}}
-action. These objects
are compact generators
of the category
D_{\text{perf}}^{\check{H}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}})
and the functor

F:=\Phi_{H}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}\rho^{\dagger}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}\Phi_{G}^{-1}

sends
Fr(V)
to a finitely generated
{\check{H}}
-equivariant

\sym{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
-module (with zero differential).
Furthermore, this functor is continuous.
It follows easily that
F
is isomorphic
to the functor
(B{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(\text{-}))^{\check{G}}
, where

B=F(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])
is an

{\check{H}}\times{\check{G}}
-equivariant dg
(S{\check{\mathfrak{h}}},S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}})
-bimodule.
Hence, using (
6.3.6
) we deduce isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]\cong\Phi_{H}(\rho^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G})

\displaystyle\cong\big(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}\,\Phi_{G}^{-1}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])\big)^{\check{G}}

\displaystyle=F(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])\cong\big(B{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])\big)^{\check{G}}\cong(B\otimes\Bbbk[{\check{G}}])^{\check{G}}\cong B.

We conclude that the functor
F
is isomorphic
to the functor
(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{H\to G}}]{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}}(\text{-}))^{\check{G}}
, and (i) follows.
∎

Step 4:  Proof of part (iii).
From (
6.3.6
) we deduce the following chain of isomorphisms

\displaystyle(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}}}\,\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G_{2}\to G_{3}}])^{\check{G}_{2}}

\displaystyle\cong\big(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}}}\,\Phi_{G_{2}}(\rho_{23}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G_{3}})\big)^{\check{G}_{2}}

\displaystyle\cong\Phi_{G_{1}}(\rho_{12}^{\dagger}\varrho_{23}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G_{3}})\cong\Phi_{G_{1}}(\rho_{13}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{R}}_{G_{3}})

\displaystyle\cong(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,\Phi_{G_{3}}({\mathcal{R}}_{G_{3}}))^{{\check{G}}_{3}}

\displaystyle\cong\big(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,(\lx@sectionsign{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}\otimes{\Bbbk}[{\check{G}}_{3})\big)^{{\check{G}}_{3}}\ \cong\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{3}}}].

This proves the isomorphism
\theta_{\text{alg}}
in (iii).

Next we observe that taking
{{\check{G}}_{3}}
-invariants, resp.

{\check{G}_{2}}
-invariants, is an exact functor on
D_{\text{perf}}^{\check{G}_{2}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3})
,
resp.
D_{\text{perf}}^{\check{G}_{2}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}})
.
It follows that for any
M\in D_{\text{perf}}^{\check{G}_{2}}(S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3})
there are canonical isomorphisms

\displaystyle(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}}}\,(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G_{2}\to G_{3}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,M)^{{\check{G}}_{3}})^{\check{G}_{2}}

\displaystyle\cong(\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{{G_{1}}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}}}\,\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G_{2}\to G_{3}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,M)^{{{\check{G}}_{3}}\times{\check{G}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\cong((\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G_{1}\to{G_{2}}}]\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}}}\,\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}}_{G_{2}\to G_{3}}])^{\check{G}_{2}}\,{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}_{S{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}_{3}}\,M)^{{\check{G}}_{3}}.

This completes the proof of (iii), hence the proof of the theorem.
∎

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
3.2.4

It was proved in
[
BFN1
]
that

H^{\text{odd}}_{{G}(O)}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{M},G})=0
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}}

is an irreducible normal variety. Moreover, the moment map

{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}}\to{\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}
for the Hamiltonian
J_{\check{G}}
-action is a
flat morphism with Lagrangian fibers and this morphism
is a
J_{\check{G}}
-torsor
away from a certain discriminant divisor in
{\mathfrak{c}}={\mathfrak{c}}_{\check{G}}
.
Further, the formula in
[
BFN1
, Theorem 4.1]
shows that the algebra

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{M},T})_{+}
is finitely generated and
it is immediate from the definition of the scheme
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{M},G}
that the fibers of the map

q_{G}:{\mathcal{Z}}_{\mathbf{M},G}\to{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
are (infinite dimesional) affine spaces.
This implies that the the cohomology of the

!
-stalk of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}
at any
T
-fixed point
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}\subseteq{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}

is a pure, rank one free
\sym{\mathfrak{t}}
-module
(note that
*
-stalks of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}}
are not well defined).
These results have been extended
in
[
BDFRT
]
to arbitrary symplectic representations which satisfy the
anomaly cancelation condition.
Thus, part (1) of the theorem now follows from Proposition
3.1.3
.

Next, we prove (3).
As has been mentioned in the proof of Theorem
1.3.2
, see Sect.
6.2
,
the morphism
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}})\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}

is étale locally isomorphic to the
morphism
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\times_{{\mathfrak{c}}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}}\to{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}
.
Hence, using (
3.2.1
) it follows by étale descent that a regular function
on
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}})
may be identified
with a function
f\in\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}]
such that
the rational function
\boldsymbol{\tau}_{{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}},\rho}^{*}(f)

is a regular function on
{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}
. Thus, we have
\Bbbk[{\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},{G}})]\cong\Bbbk[{({\mathsf{Wh}_{\check{G}}})}^{\blacktriangledown}_{\rho}]
, as desired.

We now prove (2) by
adapting the strategy used by Bellamy
[
Bel
]
in his proof that any
Coulomb branch has
symplectic singularities. Specifically, we construct a variety

Y
with symplectic singularities and a generically
étale morphism
\mathbf{M}^{!}_{G}\to Y
.
To this end, recall
the ring object
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}
for our Coulomb branch
and
let
{\mathcal{C}}:={\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbf{M},T}
be the Coulomb branch of
\mathbf{M}
, viewed as a representation
of the maximal torus
T
.
By
[
BFN2
]
, one has
{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}]\cong H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G})
,
where

\varepsilon:{\mathrm{Gr}}_{T}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}
is the imbedding. Hence, the first isomorphism
in (
6.2.3
) applied to
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}
yields an algebra isomorphism

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}))\cong({\Bbbk}[T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})]\otimes_{{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]}{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}])^{\check{T}}
; furthermore, the algebra
on the right
is finitely generated since
{\Bbbk}[T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})]
and
{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}]

are finitely generated algebras, by
[
GR
]
and
[
BFN1
]
, respectively.
We conclude that

\big({\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}}}\big)/\!/{\check{T}}:=\Spec\big(({\Bbbk}[T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})]\otimes_{{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]}{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}])^{\check{T}}\big)

is a scheme of finite type and there is an isomorphism

\Spec H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}))\cong\big({\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}}}\big)/\!/{\check{T}}.

By adjunction, we have a canonical map

H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}))\to H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G})
. Taking
W
-invariants yields a
map
H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}))^{W}\to H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G})
. This map is an injective algebra homomorphism
by Lemma
7.2.1
of the next section. Furthermore,
the induced map
\Spec H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{G(O)}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G})\to\big(\Spec H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}))\big)/W

is a birational isomorphism, by the
Localization theorem.
Thus, thanks to
[
Bel
, Lemma 2.1]
and
[
Be
, Proposition 2.4]
,
to prove that
\mathbf{M}^{!}_{G}
has symplectic singularities it suffices
to show that the scheme

\Spec H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{T(O)}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}({\mathcal{R}}_{G}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\otimes}\limits}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M},G}))/W

is an algebraic variety with symplectic singularities.

To this end, we consider
morphisms
T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})\to\widetilde{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
,
where the second morphism is the Grothendieck-Springer map
and the first morphism is a
{\check{T}}
-torsor.
Let
\widetilde{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}
, resp.
{\mathcal{X}}
, be the preimage of the open set
{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}

of regular elements
in
\widetilde{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}}
, resp.
T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})
.
It is known that there is an isomorphism
\widetilde{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{c}}\,{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}

and the complement of
{\mathcal{X}}
in
\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})}

has codimension
\geq 2
. It follows that

\widetilde{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}
, and hence
{\mathcal{X}}
, is a smooth quasi-affine variety
and one has

\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})}=\bar{\mathcal{X}}
.

Let
\mu_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}:\bar{\mathcal{X}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}
,
resp.
\mu_{{\mathcal{X}}}:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}
and
\mu_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}
,
be the moment map for the respective
Hamiltonian action of
{\check{T}}
.
It is known that the morphisms
\mu_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\mu_{\mathcal{C}}
are flat,
see
[
GR
]
,
[
BFN1
]
, and
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}

is smooth.
Thus,
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}
is a
{\check{T}}
-scheme flat over
{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}
.
Associated with this scheme
and the
{\check{T}}
-torsor
{\mathcal{X}}\to\widetilde{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}
,
there is a scheme
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}
over
\widetilde{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}
that fits into
a cartesian square in the following diagram,
were the maps indicated by ‘
{\check{T}}
’ are
{\check{T}}
-torsors:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\mathcal{C}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\check{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{t}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{{\check{T}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{{\check{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}_{r}}\times_{\mathfrak{c}}{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}

Write
{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
for the regular locus of

{\mathcal{C}}
. The morphism
\mu_{\mathcal{X}}
being smooth and quasi-affine,
so is the second projection
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
,
by smooth base change.
We deduce that
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
is a smooth quasi-affine
variety.
Observe further that
the differential of the map

\mu_{{\mathcal{X}}}-\mu_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}

is surjective at any point of
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
,
since the (anti-) diagonal action of
{\check{T}}
on
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}

is free. Hence,
one may view
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
as a Hamiltonian reduction of

{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
with respect to the
anti-diagonal
{\check{T}}
-action on
{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
. Thus, the symplectic form
on
{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
induces a symplectic form on

{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
.

The composite
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}\,\hookrightarrow\,\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}\to(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
factors through a map

{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}\to(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
,
by the universal property of geometric quotients.
It is clear that the induced pullback of the regular functions
gives an algebra map which is equal to the composition of
the following chain of algebra isomorphisms:

{\Bbbk}\big[(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}\big]\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,\big({\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}]\otimes_{{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]}{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}]\big)^{\check{T}}\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,({\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{X}}]\otimes_{{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]}{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}])^{\check{T}}\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}]^{\check{T}}\,{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\,{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}].

Here, in the second isomorphism we have used that

{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{X}}]={\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}]
and
{\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}]={\Bbbk}[{\mathcal{C}}]
, since the variety
{\mathcal{C}}
is irreducible and normal,

[
BFN1
]
, so
the complement of
{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
has codimension
{\geq 2}
.
The algebra isomorphisms above imply that the map

{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}\to(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}

induces an isomorphism
\overline{{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
.
It follows that
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}
is a smooth Zariski dense subvariety of
the scheme
(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
. Since the latter
scheme has finite type, we deduce that
(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}

is a reduced and irreducible, normal variety; moreover,
the complement of
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}^{\check{T}}{\mathcal{C}}_{reg}

has codimension
{\geq 2}
in
(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
.

To complete the proof
it remains to show, thanks to
[
Nam
, Theorem 6]
,
that
(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
is a Cohen-Macaulay
variety.

1

1

I am grateful to Tom Gannon for informing me about this result of Namikawa.

To this end, we use that
the variety
\overline{T^{*}({\check{G}}/U_{\check{G}})}=\bar{\mathcal{X}}
, resp.
{\mathcal{C}}
, has symplectic
singularities, by
[
Ga
]
, resp.
[
Bel
]
.
Hence,
\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}
has rational singularities.
Therefore,

(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
has rational singularities, by
[
Bou
]
;
in particular,
(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
is a Cohen-Macaulay variety.
From this, we will deduce that
(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
is Cohen-Macaulay
as follows.
Let
\bar{\mu}:(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}\to{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}

be the morphism
induced by the map
\mu_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}\times\mu_{\mathcal{C}}
, and let

{\mathcal{I}}_{\Delta}\subset{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]

be the ideal of the diagonal
{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}
.
Then, by definition one has

{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mu}^{-1}({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta})]={\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]^{\check{T}}\big/{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]^{\check{T}}\cdot\bar{\mu}^{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{\Delta})
, where

\bar{\mu}^{-1}({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta})
is the
scheme-theoretic

preimage of
{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta}
.
On the other hand, by Hilbert
there is an algebra isomorphism

({\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]/I_{\Delta})^{\check{T}}\,\cong\,{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]^{\check{T}}\big/{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]^{\check{T}}\cdot\bar{\mu}^{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{\Delta}),

where
I_{\Delta}
denotes
the ideal of
{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]

generated by
(\mu_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}\times\mu_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{\Delta})
.
We obtain an isomorphism

(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}=\Spec\big(({\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]/I_{\Delta})^{\check{T}}\big)\,\cong\,\bar{\mu}^{-1}({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta})
.
Now,
{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]^{\check{T}}
is a flat

{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]
-module,
since it is a direct summand of the
{\Bbbk}[{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}]
-module

{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]
, which we know is flat.
Hence, the equations of the diagonal

{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta}\subseteq{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}\times{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}
form a regular sequence in
the Cohen-Macaulay algebra

{\Bbbk}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{C}}]^{\check{T}}={\Bbbk}[(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}]
.
This implies that the scheme
\bar{\mu}^{-1}({\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}_{\Delta})\cong(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\,\times_{{\check{\mathfrak{t}}}^{*}}\,{\mathcal{C}})/\!/{\check{T}}
is also
Cohen-Macaulay, and we are done.∎

7.
Finite generation

Recall that
W
is the Weyl group and

{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
is the dominant Weyl chamber in
{\mathbb{X}}={\mathbb{X}}_{*}({T})
.
For
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, let
W_{\lambda}\subseteq W
be the stabilizer, resp.

W.\lambda
the
W
-orbit, of
\lambda
.
Let
\Sigma\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}
be the set of simple coroots and
‘
\leq
’ the standard partial order on
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}

such that
\lambda\leq\mu
if and only if
\mu-\lambda
is a sum of simple
coroots.

7.1.
Algebraic preliminaries

We begin with an elementary observation:

(7.1.1)

W_{\lambda+\mu}=W_{\lambda}\cap W_{\mu}\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}.

To see this, consider the height function

ht:{\mathbb{X}}\to{\mathbb{Z}},\,\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma}\,n_{\alpha}\alpha\mapsto\sum_{\alpha\in\Sigma}n_{\alpha}
.
For any

\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
and
w\in W\smallsetminus W_{\nu}
, one has
ht(w(\nu))<ht(\nu)
.
Therefore, for
\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, we have

(7.1.2)

\displaystyle w\not\in W_{\lambda+\mu}{\kern 5.0pt}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow{\kern 5.0pt}ht(w(\lambda+\mu))<ht(\lambda+\mu){\kern 5.0pt}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow{\kern 5.0pt}ht(w(\lambda))<ht(\lambda){\kern 5.0pt}\text{or}{\kern 5.0pt}ht(w(\mu))<ht(\mu){\kern 5.0pt}\Leftrightarrow{\kern 5.0pt}w\not\in W_{\lambda}{\kern 5.0pt}\text{or}{\kern 5.0pt}w\not\in W_{\mu}.\hfill\hskip 20.00003pt\qed

Let
B=B^{\text{even}}\oplus B^{\text{odd}}=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,B_{\lambda}^{\text{even}}\oplus B^{\text{odd}}_{\lambda}
be an
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded supercommutative
algebra
and
M=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,M_{\lambda}

an
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded

B
-module.
We say that
B
, resp.
M
, is
partially equivariant
if for each
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}

there is a linear
W_{\lambda}
-action in
B_{\lambda}
, resp.
M_{\lambda}
,
such that
w(b_{\lambda}b_{\mu})=w(b_{\lambda})w(b_{\mu})
,
resp.
w(b_{\lambda}m_{\mu})=w(b_{\lambda})w(m_{\mu})
, for all
w\in W_{\lambda+\mu},\,b_{\lambda}\in B_{\lambda},\,b_{\mu}\in B_{\mu}
, resp.
m_{\mu}\in M_{\mu}
.
These equations make sense thanks (
7.1.1
).
We define

B^{\flat}:=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,(B_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}},{\kern 5.0pt}\text{resp.}{\kern 5.0pt}M^{\flat}:=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,(M_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}.

It follows from (
7.1.1
) that one has an inclusion

(B_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}\,(M_{\mu})^{W_{\mu}}\subseteq(M_{\lambda+\mu})^{W_{\lambda+\mu}}
.
We deduce (taking
M=B
) that
B^{\flat}
is a subalgebra of
B
,
resp.
M^{\flat}
is an
B^{\flat}
-submodule of
M
.

Proposition 7.1.3
.

If the algebra
B
is finitely generated then
B^{\flat}

is a finitely generated algebra and
B
is a finitely generated

B^{\flat}
-module.

The proof of the proposision mimics the classic argument due to Hilbert as follows.
Let
BM^{\flat}\subseteq M
be the
B
-submodule generated by
M^{\flat}
.
We start with

Claim 7.1.4
.

If
m_{i}\in(M_{\mu_{i}})^{W_{\mu_{i}}},\,i=1,\ldots,n
, generate

BM^{\flat}
as a
B
-module then these elements
generate
M^{\flat}
as an
B^{\flat}
-module.

Proof of Claim.

Let
\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
and
{\sigma}_{\nu}:M\to M^{W_{\nu}}
be the averaging operator

m\mapsto\frac{1}{|W_{\nu}|}\sum_{w\in W_{\nu}}\,w(m)
.
Since the elements
m_{i}
generate
BM^{\flat}
,
any element
m_{\nu}\in M_{\nu}\cap(BM^{\flat})

can be written in the form

m_{\nu}:=b_{1}m_{1}+\ldots b_{n}m_{n}\in M_{\nu}
for some

\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}

such that
\lambda_{i}+\mu_{i}=\nu
for all
i
and some
b_{i}\in B_{\lambda_{i}},\,i=1,\ldots,n
.
Observe that (
7.1.1
) implies that for all
i
the group
W_{\nu}
is
contained in
W_{\mu_{i}}
, so we have
{\sigma}_{\nu}(m_{i})=m_{i}
for all
i
.
In the special case where
m_{\nu}\in(M_{\nu})^{W_{\nu}}=M_{\nu}\cap M^{\flat}
, we find

m_{\nu}={\sigma}_{\nu}(m_{\nu})={\sigma}_{\nu}(b_{1})m_{1}+\ldots{\sigma}_{\nu}(b_{n})m_{n}\in B^{\flat}m_{1}+\ldots+B^{\flat}m_{n},

and our claim follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.1.3
.

Assume that the algebra
B
is finitely generated.
Let
I:=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}}\,B_{\lambda}
,
resp.
B^{\flat}\cap I=I^{\flat}=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}}\,(B_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}
,
be the augmentation ideal of
B
, resp.
B^{\flat}
.
To prove that
B^{\flat}
is a finitely generated algebra, it suffices
to show that
I^{\flat}

is a finitely generated ideal (this statement holds for supercommutative algebras
with the same proof as in the case of commutative algebras).
Since
B
is noetherian the ideal
B.I^{\flat}
, of
B
, is
generated by finitely many elements
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}
,
which we may assume without loss of generality
to be elements of
I^{\flat}
. Applying
Claim
7.1.4
in the case where
M=I

we deduce that the elements
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}

generate the ideal
I^{\flat}
,
as desired.

It remains to prove that
B
is finitely generated as a
B^{\flat}
-module.
To this end, let
\Bbbk W
be the group algebra of
W
viewed as the
regular representation of

W
. We make

\Bbbk W\otimes B=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,\Bbbk W\otimes B_{\lambda}
a partially equivariant
B
-module by letting

B
act on
\Bbbk W\otimes B
by
{}_{1}:w\otimes b_{2}\mapsto w\otimes b_{1}b_{2}
and
letting
W_{\lambda}
act in the direct summand
\Bbbk W\otimes B_{\lambda}
by

w^{\prime}:w\otimes b\mapsto w(w^{\prime})^{-1}\otimes w^{\prime}(b)
.
Thus,
\Bbbk W\otimes B
is a free
B
-module of rank
|W|

and
(\Bbbk W\otimes B)^{\flat}:=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,(\Bbbk W\otimes B_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}
is
a
B^{\flat}
-submodule of that module.
The
B
-submodule of
\Bbbk W\otimes B
generated by

(\Bbbk W\otimes B)^{\flat}
is finitely generated,
since
B
is noetherian. Therefore,
(\Bbbk W\otimes B)^{\flat}

is a finitely generated
B^{\flat}
-module, by
Claim
7.1.4
.
The vector space
\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,(\Bbbk W_{\lambda}\otimes B_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}\cong\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,B_{\lambda}
is
a
B^{\flat}
-submodule of
(\Bbbk W\otimes B_{+})^{\flat}
isomorphic to
B
.
Thus, this submodule is finitely generated, since we proved that

B^{\flat}
is finitely generated, hence noetherian.
∎

Remark 7.1.5
.

Let
\Bbbk{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
be
the semigroup algebra of
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
.
The
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-grading gives a
T
-action on
B
and this action extends to an
action of the algebraic monoid
\bar{T}=\Spec(\Bbbk{\mathbb{X}}_{+})
.

\lozenge

Next, let
A=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,A_{\lambda}
, resp.
M=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}}\,M_{\lambda}
,
be an
{\mathbb{X}}
-graded algebra, resp.

A
-module,
equipped with a
W
-action by algebra, resp.
A
-module, automorphisms
such that
w(A_{\lambda})\subseteq A_{w(\lambda)}
, resp.
w(M_{\lambda})\subseteq M_{w(\lambda)}
,
for all
w\in W
.
Then,
A_{+}=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,A_{\lambda}
, resp.
M_{+}=\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,M_{\lambda}
, is a
partially equivariant algebra, resp.
A_{+}
-module.
Thus, one has the subalgebra
A_{+}^{\flat}:=(A_{+})^{\flat}\subseteq A_{+}
,
resp.
A_{+}^{\flat}
-submodule
M_{+}^{\flat}:=(M_{+})^{\flat}\subseteq M_{+}
.

Corollary 7.1.6
.

Assume that the semigroup
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
is free.
If
A
is an integrally closed domain then so is
A_{+}
; in this case,

\Spec A_{+}\to\Spec A_{+}^{\flat}
is the normalization map.

Proof.
2
2

2

I am grateful to Tom Gannon for providing this proof.

The first statement is a consequence of the following more general claim:
let
X
be a free abelian group with basis
\omega_{1},\ldots\omega_{\ell}

and

A=\oplus_{\lambda\in X}A_{\lambda}
an
X
-graded integrally closed domain.
Then the algebra
A_{+}:=\oplus_{\lambda\in X_{+}}A_{\lambda}
, where
X_{+}
is the free semigroup
generated by
\omega_{1},\ldots\omega_{\ell}
, is integrally closed.
Using induction on
\ell
the claim may be reduced to the case
\ell=1
.
Thus, we may (and will) assume that
X={\mathbb{Z}}
, so
A=\oplus_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\,A_{n}
.

Since
A
is integrally closed, it suffices to prove that if
b\in\text{Frac}(A_{+})\cap A

satisfies an equation
f(b)=0
for some monic polynomial

f(t)=t^{d}+f_{d-1}t^{d-1}+\ldots+f_{0}\in A_{+}[t]
, then
b\in A_{+}
.
We may write

b=p+q
for
q\in A_{+}
and

p=p_{-m}+p_{-m+1}+\ldots+p_{-1}
, where
p_{i}\in A_{i}
.
If
b\not\in A_{+}
we may assume without loss of generality that

p_{-m}\neq 0
.
Expanding the equation
f(p+q)=0
we find that

p^{d}+dp^{d-1}q+\ldots+q^{d}+f_{d-1}p^{d-1}+\ldots+f_{d-1}q^{d-1}+\ldots+f_{0}=0
.
The homogeneous component of the LHS of degree
-md

equals
(p_{-m})^{d}
. Thus, the equation implies that
p_{-m}=0
, a contradiction.

To complete the proof, recall that
A_{+}
is a finitely generated
A_{+}^{\flat}
-module,
by Proposition
7.1.3
. Hence, it remains to show that the inclusion
\text{Frac}(A_{+}^{\flat})\,\hookrightarrow\,\text{Frac}(A_{+})
is an equality.
To this end,
let
a=\sum_{\nu\in S}\,a_{\nu}\in A_{+}
, where
a_{\nu}\in A_{\nu}
and
S
is a finite subset of
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
.
Pick a nonzero element

q\in A_{\lambda}
such that
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
is sufficiently general so that
W_{\lambda}=\{1\}
.
By (
7.1.1
), for all
\nu\in S
we have
W_{\nu+\lambda}=\{1\}
.
Therefore,
q
and all elements
a_{\nu}\cdot q
are in
A_{+}^{\flat}
.
Hence, we have
a=\sum_{\nu\in S}\,\frac{a_{\nu}\cdot q}{q}\in\text{Frac}(A_{+}^{\flat})
.
We conclude that

A_{+}\subseteq\text{Frac}(A_{+}^{\flat})
, which implies that
\text{Frac}(A_{+}^{\flat})=\text{Frac}(A_{+})
.
∎

In the above setting, for each
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
let

(A_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}\to A^{W}
, resp.

(M_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}\to M^{W}
, be
the symmetrization map
x\mapsto\frac{1}{|W|}\sum_{w\in W}\,w(x)
.
It is clear that these maps yield a bijection
{\sigma}:A^{\flat}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}A^{W}
,
resp.
{\sigma}:M^{\flat}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}M^{W}
.

We will need the following simple result that
has been essentially observed in
[
BFN1
]
.

Lemma 7.1.7
.

For any
a\in(A_{\lambda})^{W_{\lambda}}
and
m\in(M_{\mu})^{W_{\mu}}
, where

\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, one has

{\sigma}(am)={\sigma}(a){\sigma}(m)+\sum_{\nu}\,{\sigma}(m_{\nu})
,
for some
\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
such that
\nu<\lambda+\mu
and

m_{\nu}\in(M_{\nu})^{W_{\nu}}
.

Proof.

Write

W\times W=(W_{\lambda}\times W_{\mu})\sqcup{\mathscr{S}}
where

{\mathscr{S}}=(W\smallsetminus W_{\lambda})\times W\,\bigcup\,(W\times(W\smallsetminus W_{\mu})
.
From the definition of
{\sigma}
one easily finds

{\sigma}(a){\sigma}(m)-{\sigma}(am)\ =\frac{1}{|W|^{2}}\sum_{(w,y)\in{\mathscr{S}}}\,w(a)\,y(m).

The sum on the right is in the image of the map
{\sigma}
since

{\sigma}(b){\sigma}(m)-{\sigma}(bm)\in M^{W}=\im({\sigma})
.
The desired statement now follows from the observation
that for any
(w,y)\in{\mathscr{S}}
, by (
7.1.2
) , one has

w(\lambda)+y(\mu)<\lambda+\mu
.
∎

For any
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
let

{\mathbb{X}}_{\unlhd\lambda}:=\bigsqcup_{\{\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}\,\mid\,\mu\leq\lambda\}}\,W.\mu
.
We write
\nu\unlhd\lambda
, resp.
\nu\lhd\lambda
,
if
\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\unlhd\lambda}
, resp.
\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\unlhd\lambda}\smallsetminus W.\lambda
.
Given an
{\mathbb{X}}
-graded algebra
A
with a
W
-action, as above,
we define an ascending filtration
A_{\unlhd\lambda},\,\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, on
A
,
by the formula
A_{\unlhd\lambda}=\bigoplus_{\{\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}\,\mid\,\mu\unlhd\lambda\}}\,A_{\mu}
.
The space
A_{\unlhd\lambda}
is clearly
W
-stable. It follows from
Lemma
7.1.7
that for all
\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}

one has
(A_{\unlhd\lambda})^{W}\cdot(A_{\unlhd\mu})^{W}\subseteq(A_{\unlhd\lambda+\mu})^{W}
;
moreover, the map
{\sigma}
induces an isomorphism

A^{\flat}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,(A_{\unlhd\lambda})^{W}/(A_{\lhd\lambda})^{W}
,
of
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded algebras.
This, combined with Proposition
7.1.3
, yields the following result.

Corollary 7.1.8
.

If the algebra
A_{+}
is finitely generated then so is the algebra
A^{W}
.
∎

7.2.
Filtration by dominant weights

In this subsection we will
exploit an idea from
[
BFN1
]
to prove Proposition
7.2.3
below,
which is a key step of the proof of
Proposition
6.2.1
.
We will use simplified notation

{\mathsf{H}}(\text{-})=H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{G}(O)}(\text{-})
, resp.

{\mathbb{H}}(\text{-})=H^{\>\raisebox{2.0pt}{\text{\circle*{1.5}}}}_{{T}(O)}(\text{-})

and write
{\mathfrak{t}}=\Lie{T}
, resp.
{\mathrm{S}}=\Bbbk[{\mathfrak{t}}]={\mathbb{H}}({\operatorname{pt}})
and

{\mathsf{C}}={\mathrm{S}}^{W}={\mathsf{H}}({\operatorname{pt}})
.

Recall the
imbedding
\varepsilon:{\mathbb{X}}={\mathrm{Gr}}_{T}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathrm{Gr}}={\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}

and the restriction functor
\varepsilon^{\dagger}:D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})\to D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{T})
.
For any
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
there is a natural
W
-action on

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
such that
w(sh)=w(s)w(h)
for all

s\in{\mathrm{S}}
and
h\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
.
If
{\mathcal{F}}
is a ring object then so is
\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}
and the group
W
acts on the ring

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
by ring automorphisms. Thus,

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})^{W}
is a
{\mathsf{C}}
-subalgebra of
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
.

Lemma 7.2.1
.

For any commutative ring object
{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})

the adjunction
\varepsilon_{!}:{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})^{W}\to{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})

is a
{\mathsf{C}}
-algebra homomorphism.

Proof.

The argument below is similar to the one used in the proof
of
[
BFN1
, Lemma 5.10]
.
To simplify notation, for
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}

we write
\bar{\mathcal{F}}=\text{Obl}^{{G}(O)}_{{T}(O)}{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
.

We have the following natural maps

{T}(K)\times_{{T}(O)}{\mathrm{Gr}}\xrightarrow{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{G}(K)\times_{{G}(O)}{\mathrm{Gr}}\xrightarrow{a}{\mathrm{Gr}}
.
Let
\tilde{a}=a{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}\tilde{\varepsilon}
denote the composite
and given
{\mathcal{E}}\in D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{T})
, resp.
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
,
let
{\mathcal{E}}\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{T(O)}\,\mathcal{G}\in{T}(K)\times_{{T}(O)}{\mathrm{Gr}}
,
resp.
\mathcal{G}\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{G(O)}{\mathcal{F}}\in{G}(K)\times_{{G}(O)}{\mathrm{Gr}}
,
denote the descent of
{\mathcal{E}}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}
, resp.
\mathcal{G}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}
.
One makes
D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
a left, resp. right, module category
over the monoidal category
D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{T})
, resp.
D_{G(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
,
by letting
{\mathcal{E}}\in D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{T})
, resp.
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
,
act on
D_{{T}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})
by the formula

{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{G}\mapsto{\mathcal{E}}\star_{\ell}\mathcal{G}:=\tilde{a}_{!}({\mathcal{E}}\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{T(O)}\,\mathcal{G})
,
resp.
{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{G}\mapsto\mathcal{G}\star_{r}{\mathcal{F}}=a_{!}(\mathcal{G}\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{G(O)}{\mathcal{F}})
.

For
{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
, using the natural isomorphism
(\varepsilon^{!}\bar{\mathcal{E}})\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{T(O)}\,\bar{\mathcal{F}}\cong\tilde{\varepsilon}^{!}(\bar{\mathcal{E}}\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{G(O)}\,{\mathcal{F}})

we obtain a chain of
morphisms

(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{E}})\star_{\ell}\bar{\mathcal{F}}=\tilde{a}_{!}((\varepsilon^{!}\bar{\mathcal{E}})\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{T(O)}\,\bar{\mathcal{F}}){\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}a_{!}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{!}\tilde{\varepsilon}^{!}(\bar{\mathcal{E}}\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{G(O)}\,{\mathcal{F}})\xrightarrow{}a_{!}(\bar{\mathcal{E}}\,\widetilde{\boxtimes}_{G(O)}\,{\mathcal{F}})=\bar{\mathcal{E}}\star_{r}{\mathcal{F}}=\overline{{\mathcal{E}}\star{\mathcal{F}}}.

Restricting the map

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{E}})\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}{\mathbb{H}}(\bar{\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathbb{H}}(\overline{{\mathcal{E}}\star{\mathcal{F}}})
induced by the composite morphism
to
W
-invariants yields a map

*_{\ell}:{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{E}})^{W}\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{E}}\star{\mathcal{F}})
.
Tracing through the construction one obtains the following equation:

(7.2.2)

h*_{\ell}h^{\prime}=\varepsilon_{!}(h)\star h^{\prime},\hskip 20.00003pt\forall h\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{E}})^{W},{\kern 5.0pt}h^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}}).

To complete the proof we recall that the unit of the ring object
{\mathcal{F}}

is a morphism
{\boldsymbol{1}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
subject to a certain compatibility.
Let
1
be
the image of the unit of the algebra
{\mathsf{C}}
under the induced
map
{\mathsf{C}}={\mathsf{H}}({\boldsymbol{1}})\to{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
.
We use the same symbol
1
for the element

1\otimes 1\in{\mathrm{S}}\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathbb{H}}(\bar{\mathcal{F}})
.
It is not difficult to see that for
h\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})
, in
{\mathbb{H}}(\bar{\mathcal{F}})
,
one has
h*_{\ell}1=\varepsilon_{!}(h)
.
Thus, using (
7.2.2
) we obtain

\varepsilon_{!}(h\star h^{\prime})=(h\star h^{\prime})*_{\ell}1=h*_{\ell}(h^{\prime}*_{\ell}1)=h*_{\ell}\varepsilon_{!}(h^{\prime})=\varepsilon_{!}(h)\star\varepsilon_{!}(h^{\prime}),\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\,h,h^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})^{W}.\qed

For
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, let
{i}_{\leq\lambda}
, resp.
i_{<\lambda}
, denote the closed imbedding
of
{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}
, resp.
{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}\smallsetminus{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}
, into
{\mathrm{Gr}}
.
If
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
is
!
-pure then it follows from
Corollary
4.1.7
that for all
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}

the maps
({i}_{\leq\lambda})_{!}:{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})

are injective. Thus, the images of the maps
({i}_{\leq\lambda})_{!}

give an ascending filtration of
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
.
From now on, we will identify
{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})
with its image.

Proposition 7.2.3
.

For any
!
-pure commutative ring object
{\mathcal{F}}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}

the filtration
{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}),\,\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, on the ring
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})

is multiplicative and
there is an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded
{\mathsf{C}}
-algebras:

\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})/{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{<\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})\,\cong\,\big((H(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}}))_{+}\big)^{\flat}.

To prove the proposition, fix
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, identify it with the
corresponding
T
-fixed point
of
({\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda})^{T}
, and let

{G}(O)_{\lambda}\subseteq{G}(O)
be the stabilizer of that point in
{G}(O)
.
Then,
P_{\lambda}={G}\cap{G}(O)_{\lambda}
is a parabolic subgroup of
{G}
,
the imbedding
{G}/P_{\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,{G}(O)/{G}(O)_{\lambda}\cong{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}
is a

{G}
-equivariant homotopy
equivalence and there are

{\mathsf{C}}
-algebra isomorphisms

{\mathsf{H}}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda})\cong{\mathsf{H}}({G}/P_{\lambda})\cong{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}
.

Recall the partial order
\unlhd
on
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
defined at the end of
Sect.
7.1
. The imbedding
\varepsilon
restricts
to an imbedding

\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda}:{\mathbb{X}}_{\unlhd\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}
,
resp.
\epsilon_{\lhd\lambda}:{\mathbb{X}}_{\lhd\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}
,
with image
({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})^{T}
, resp.
({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}\smallsetminus{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda})^{T}
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}\in D_{{G}(O)}({\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}})
be a
!
-pure object and

{\mathcal{L}}_{<\lambda}
, resp.
{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}
, the
!
-restriction of
{\mathcal{L}}

along the closed, resp. open, imbedding

{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}\smallsetminus{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}
, resp.
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\overline{{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\lambda}}}}
.
We have a direct sum decomposition
{\mathbb{H}}(\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda}^{!}{\mathcal{L}})=\oplus_{\nu\unlhd\lambda}\,{\mathbb{H}}_{\nu}({\mathcal{L}})
, where
{\mathbb{H}}_{\nu}({\mathcal{L}})
is the

{T}
-equivariant cohomology
of the stalk of
\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda}^{!}{\mathcal{L}}
at
\nu
.
There are natural
{\mathsf{C}}
-linear maps

(7.2.4)

{\mathbb{H}}_{\lambda}({\mathcal{L}})^{W_{\lambda}}\,\xrightarrow{\cong}\,\big(\oplus_{\nu\in W.\lambda}\,{\mathbb{H}}_{\nu}({\mathcal{L}})\big)^{W}\,\hookrightarrow\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon_{\unlhd\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{L}})^{W}\xrightarrow{\ (\varepsilon_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}\ }{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}),

where the first map is the symmetrization map

h\mapsto\{w(h)\in{\mathbb{H}}_{w(\lambda)}({\mathcal{L}})\}_{w\in W/W_{\lambda}}
.
Let
{\sigma}_{\unlhd\lambda}
be the composite of the first two maps in (
7.2.4
).

By
Lemma
4.1.4

there is a short exact sequence

(7.2.5)

0\to{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{<\lambda})\xrightarrow{}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}})\xrightarrow{\ \text{res}\ }{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda})\to 0

We obtain the following chain of
{\mathsf{C}}
-linear maps

(7.2.6)

{\mathbb{H}}_{\lambda}({\mathcal{L}})^{W_{\lambda}}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\ \bar{\sigma}\ }{\mathbb{H}}(\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{L}})^{W}/{\mathbb{H}}(\epsilon_{\lhd\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{L}})^{W}\xrightarrow{(\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}})/{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{<\lambda})\xrightarrow[\cong]{\ \overline{\text{res}}\ }{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}).

where
(\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}
is the map induced by
\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}

and

\bar{\sigma}
is the composite of
{\sigma}_{\unlhd\lambda}

and the quotient map.
The natural action of
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda})\cong{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}
gives
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda})

the structure of an
{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}
-module.

Let
Q=\text{Frac}({\mathrm{S}})

be the fraction field
of
{\mathrm{S}}
.
Given an
{\mathrm{S}}
-module, resp.
{\mathsf{C}}
-module,

M
,
we put
QM=Q\otimes_{\mathrm{S}}M
, resp.

Q^{W}M:=Q^{W}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}}M

and
Q^{W_{\lambda}}M=Q^{W_{\lambda}}\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}M
.
Similarly, given a morphism
f:M\to N
of
{\mathrm{S}}
-modules,
we have a morphism
Qf:QM\to QN
of
Q
-modules, etc.
Bellow, all localization maps like
M\to QM
are injective, thanks to
the
!
-purity of
{\mathcal{L}}
.

We extend the map
{\sigma}_{\unlhd\lambda}
, resp.
(\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}
,

(\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}
and
\overline{\text{res}}
,
to a
Q^{W}
-linear map
Q^{W}{\sigma}_{\unlhd\lambda}
, resp.
Q^{W}(\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}
,

Q^{W}(\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}
and
Q^{W}\overline{\text{res}}
.
The composite

Q^{W}(\overline{\text{res}}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}(\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda})_{!}):Q^{W}{\mathbb{H}}(\epsilon_{\unlhd\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{L}})^{W}/Q^{W}{\mathbb{H}}(\epsilon_{\lhd\lambda}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{L}})^{W}\to Q^{W}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda})

is an isomorphism of
Q^{W}
-modules, by the Localization theorem.
Let
q_{\lambda}
be the composite map
in (
7.2.6
). This map is

{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}
-linear and it follows from the above that
the map
Q^{W_{\lambda}}q_{\lambda}:Q^{W_{\lambda}}{\mathbb{H}}_{\lambda}({\mathcal{L}})^{W_{\lambda}}\to Q^{W_{\lambda}}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda})
is an isomorphism
of
Q^{W_{\lambda}}
-modules.

Let
\Delta^{\text{pos}}\in{\mathbb{X}}^{*}(T)
be the set of positive roots associated
with our choice
\Sigma\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}_{*}(T)
of the set of simple coroots.
Following
[
BFN1
]
, for
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
we define

{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}:=\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta^{\text{pos}}}\ \alpha^{\langle\lambda,\alpha\rangle}\in{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}.

Lemma 7.2.7
.

The image of the map

\,{\mathbb{H}}_{\lambda}({\mathcal{L}})^{W_{\lambda}}\to Q{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}),\ h\mapsto\mbox{$\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}}$}(Q\bar{\sigma}(q_{\lambda}(h))
,
equals the
subspace
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda})\subset Q{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda})
; furthermore, the resulting
map
yields an isomorphism of
{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}
-modules:

\mbox{$\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}}$}\bar{\sigma}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}q_{\lambda}:\,{\mathbb{H}}_{\lambda}({\mathcal{L}})^{W_{\lambda}}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}).

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{L}}
is
!
-pure, in
D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda})
there is an isomorphism

{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}\cong\oplus_{m}\,{\mathcal{H}}^{m}{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}[-m]
; moreover,

{\mathcal{H}}^{m}{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}

are geometrically constant sheaves on
{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\lambda}
.
Therefore, it suffices to prove the lemma in the case where
{\mathcal{L}}_{\lambda}
is a constant sheaf.
This is, essentially, the content of the proof
of

[
BFN1
, Proposition 6.2]
. In that proof,
{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}

appears
as an equivariant Euler class.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.2.3
.

For any
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}\in{D_{{G}(O)}({\mathrm{Gr}})}
and
\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
, the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\ {\mathsf{H}}(i_{\leq\lambda}^{!}\mathcal{G}_{1})\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}{\mathsf{H}}(i_{\leq\mu}^{!}\mathcal{G}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\star}
\scriptstyle{({i}_{\leq\lambda})_{!}\otimes({i}_{\leq\mu})_{!}}
\textstyle{\ {\mathsf{H}}(\mathcal{G}_{1})\otimes_{\mathsf{C}}{\mathsf{H}}(\mathcal{G}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\star}
\textstyle{\ {\mathsf{H}}(i_{\leq\lambda+\mu}^{!}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\star\mathcal{G}_{2}))\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({i}_{\leq\lambda+\mu})_{!}}
\textstyle{\ {\mathsf{H}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\star\mathcal{G}_{2})}

Now let
{\mathcal{F}}
be the ring object from the statement of the proposition.
From the commutative diagram above we deduce that the filtration
{\mathsf{H}}(i_{\lambda}^{!}{\mathcal{F}})

on
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
is multiplicative.
Further,
for each
\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
we have an isomorphism

\mbox{$\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}}$}\bar{\sigma}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}q_{\lambda}:\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})^{W_{\lambda}}{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}{\mathsf{H}}(i_{\lambda}^{!}{\mathcal{F}})
,
by Lemma
7.2.7
applied in the case
{\mathcal{L}}=i^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}
.
Therefore, the assignment
h\mapsto\zeta(h):=\overline{\text{res}}^{-1}(\mbox{$\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}}$}\bar{\sigma}{{}_{\,{}^{{}^{\circ}}}}q_{\lambda})(h)
gives a
{\mathsf{C}}
-module isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})^{W_{\lambda}}

{\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}{\mathsf{H}}(i^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})/{\mathsf{H}}(i^{!}_{<\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})
.
Assembling these isomorphisms for all
\lambda
we obtain an isomorphism
of
{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
-graded
{\mathsf{C}}
-modules

\zeta:\ {\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger})_{+}^{\flat}=\mbox{$\bigoplus$}_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})^{W_{\lambda}}\ {\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\to}\limits}}\;}\ \oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,{\mathsf{H}}(i^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})/{\mathsf{H}}(i^{!}_{<\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}).

It remains to prove that the map
\zeta
is a ring homomorphism.
To this end, we observe that the assignment

h\mapsto{\mathfrak{d}}_{\nu}\cdot h
gives an injective map

{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\nu}{\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\nu}{\mathcal{F}})
,
resp.
{\mathsf{H}}(i^{!}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathsf{H}}(i^{!}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})
,
since this cohomology group is a free
{\mathrm{S}}^{W_{\lambda}}
-module.
For
h\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\nu}{\mathcal{F}})
, by construction, we have

{\mathfrak{d}}_{\nu}\cdot\zeta(h)=\zeta({\mathfrak{d}}_{\nu}\cdot h)=\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\nu}(\bar{\sigma}(h)).

Observe, that we have
{\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda+\mu}={\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}\cdot{\mathfrak{d}}_{\mu}
for all
\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
.
Thus, it suffices to show that
for all
a\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})^{W_{\lambda}}
and
b\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\mu}{\mathcal{F}})^{W_{\mu}}
,
one has

\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda+\mu}(\bar{\sigma}(ab))=\zeta({\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda+\mu}\cdot ab)\ \mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\ \zeta({\mathfrak{d}}_{\lambda}\cdot a)\cdot\zeta({\mathfrak{d}}_{\mu}\cdot b)=\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\lambda}(\bar{\sigma}(a))\cdot\bar{\epsilon}_{\unlhd\mu}(\bar{\sigma}(b)).

Explicitly, this means that we must show that the element

\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(ab))-\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(a))\cdot\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(b))

is contained in
{\mathsf{H}}(i_{<\lambda+\mu}^{!}{\mathcal{F}})
.
Using Lemma
7.2.1
and the notation of Lemma
7.1.3
, we find

\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(ab))-\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(a))\cdot\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(b))=\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(ab)-{\sigma}(a){\sigma}(b))=\sum_{\{\nu\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}\,\mid\,\nu<\lambda+\mu\}}\,\varepsilon_{!}({\sigma}(m_{\nu})),

for some

m_{\nu}\in{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{\nu}{\mathcal{F}})
. The map
\varepsilon_{!}
sends

(\oplus_{\{w(\nu),\,w\in W\}}\,{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}_{w(\nu)}{\mathcal{F}})^{W}

to
{\mathsf{H}}(i_{\leq\nu}^{!}{\mathcal{F}})
. We conclude that
the sum in the right hand side of the displayed formula is contained
in
{\mathsf{H}}(i_{<\lambda+\mu}^{!}{\mathcal{F}})
. This proves our claim, and
Proposition
7.2.3
follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition Proposition
6.2.1
.

Assume that the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})_{+}
is finitely generated.
Then, the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}(\varepsilon^{\dagger}{\mathcal{F}})^{W}
, resp.
\oplus_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}}\,{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})/{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{<\lambda}{\mathcal{F}})
,
is finitely generated by Corollary
7.1.8
, resp.
Proposition
7.2.3
. Since the spaces
{\mathsf{H}}({i}^{!}_{\leq\lambda}{\mathcal{F}}),\,\lambda\in{\mathbb{X}}_{+}
,
form a multiplicative
filtration on
{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
, we deduce that the algebra

{\mathsf{H}}({\mathcal{F}})
is also finitely generated.
∎
